ALL IS TRUE
([[characters/henry-viii|HENRY VIII]])
BY WILLIAM SHAKESPEARE AND JOHN FLETCHER
ON 29 June 1613 the firing of cannon at the Globe Theatre ignited its thatch and burned it to the ground. According to a letter of 4 July the house was full of spectators who had come to see ‘a new play called All is True, which had been acted not passing two or three times before’. No one was hurt ‘except one man who was scalded with the [[motifs/fire|fire]] by adventuring in to save a [[archetypes/child|child]] which otherwise had been burnt’. This establishes the play’s date with unusual precision. Though two other accounts of the [[motifs/fire|fire]] refer to a play ‘of’—which may mean simply ‘about’—[[characters/henry-viii|Henry VIII]], yet another two unequivocally call it All is True; and these words also end the refrain of a ballad about the [[motifs/fire|fire]]. When the play came to be printed as the last of the English [[concepts/history|history]] plays—all named after kings—in the 1623 Folio it was as The Famous [[concepts/history|History]] of the Life of [[archetypes/king|King]] Henry the Eighth. We restore the title by which it was known to its first audiences.
No surviving account of the [[motifs/fire|fire]] says who wrote the play that caused it. In 1850, James Spedding (prompted by Tennyson) suggested that Shakespeare collaborated on it with John Fletcher (1579-1625). We have external evidence that the two dramatists worked together in or around 1613 on the lost [[Cardenio]] and on The Two Noble Kinsmen. For their collaboration in All is True the evidence is wholly internal, stemming from the initial perception of two distinct verse styles within the play; later, more rigorous examination of evidence provided by both the play’s language and its dramatic technique has convinced most scholars of Fletcher’s hand in it. The passages most confidently attributed to Shakespeare are Act 1, Scenes 1 and 2; Act 2, Scenes 3 and 4; Act 3, Scene 2 to line 204; and Act 5, Scene 1.
The historical material derives, often closely, from the chronicles of Raphael Holinshed and Edward Hall, supplemented by John Foxe’s Book of Martyrs (1563, etc.) for the [[characters/cranmer|Cranmer]] episodes in Act 5. It covers only part of Henry’s reign, from the opening description of the Field of the Cloth of Gold, of 1520, to the christening of Princess Elizabeth, in 1533. It depicts the increasing abuse of power by [[characters/cardinal-wolsey|Cardinal Wolsey]]; the execution, brought about by Wolsey’s machinations, of the [[characters/duke-of-buckingham|Duke of Buckingham]]; the [[archetypes/king|King]]’s abandonment of his [[characters/queen|Queen]], Katherine of Aragon; the rise to the [[archetypes/king|King]]’s favour of Anne Boleyn; Wolsey’s disgrace; and the birth to Henry and Anne of a daughter instead of the hoped-for son.
Sir Henry Wotton, writing of the [[motifs/fire|fire]], said that the play represented ‘some principal pieces of the reign of Henry 8, which was set forth with many extraordinary circumstances of pomp and majesty’. It has continued popular in performance for the opportunities that it affords for spectacle and for the dramatic power of certain episodes such as [[characters/buckingham|Buckingham]]’s speeches before execution (2.1), [[characters/queen-katherine|Queen Katherine]]’s defence of the validity of her [[motifs/marriage|marriage]] (2.4), Wolsey’s farewell to his [[concepts/greatness|greatness]] (3.2), and Katherine’s dying scene (4.2). Though the play depicts a series of falls from [[concepts/greatness|greatness]], it works towards the birth of the future Elizabeth I, fulsomely celebrated in the last scene (not attributed to Shakespeare) along with her successor, the patron of the [[archetypes/king|King]]’s Men.

THE PERSONS OF THE PLAY

All Is True
Prologue
Enter Prologue
PROLOGUE
I come no more to make you laugh. Things now
That bear a weighty and a serious brow,
Sad, high, and working, full of state and woe—
Such noble scenes as draw the eye to flow
We now present. Those that can pity here
May, if they think it [[motifs/well|well]], let fall a tear.
The subject will deserve it. Such as give
Their money out of [[concepts/hope|hope]] they may believe,
May here find [[concepts/truth|truth]], too. Those that come to see
Only a show or two, and so agree
The play may pass, if they be still, and willing,
I’ll undertake may see away their shilling
Richly in two short hours. Only they
That come to hear a merry bawdy play,
A noise of targets, or to see a fellow
In a long motley coat guarded with yellow,
Will be deceived. For, gentle hearers, know
To rank our chosen [[concepts/truth|truth]] with such a show
As [[archetypes/fool|fool]] and fight is, beside forfeiting
Our own brains, and the opinion that we bring
To make that only true we now intend,
Will leave us never an understanding friend.
Therefore, for goodness’ sake, and as you are known
The first and happiest hearers of the town,
Be sad as we would make ye. Think ye see
The very persons of our noble story
As they were living; think you see them great,
And followed with the general throng and sweat
Of thousand friends; then, in a moment, see
How soon this mightiness meets misery. 30
And if you can be merry then, I’ll say
A man may weep upon his wedding day.
Exit

| *(didascalia)* | A cloth of state throughout the play. Enter the Duke of Norfolk at one door; at the other door enter the [[characters/duke-of-buckingham\|Duke of Buckingham]] and the Lord Abergavenny |
| [[characters/buckingham\|BUCKINGHAM]] | (to Norfolk)<br>Good morrow, and [[motifs/well\|well]] met. How have ye done<br>Since last we saw in France? |
| NORFOLK | I thank your grace,<br>Healthful, and ever since a fresh admirer<br>Of what I saw there. |
| [[characters/buckingham\|BUCKINGHAM]] | An untimely ague<br>Stayed me a prisoner in my chamber when 5<br>Those suns of glory, those two lights of men,<br>Met in the vale of Ardres. |
| NORFOLK | ’Twixt Guisnes and Ardres.<br>I was then present, saw them salute on horseback,<br>Beheld them when they lighted, how they clung<br>In their embracement as they grew together,<br>Which had they, what four throned ones could have<br>weighed<br>Such a compounded one? |
| [[characters/buckingham\|BUCKINGHAM]] | All the whole [[motifs/time\|time]]<br>I was my chamber’s prisoner. |
| NORFOLK | Then you lost<br>The view of earthly glory. Men might say<br>Till this [[motifs/time\|time]] pomp was single, but now married<br>To one above itself. Each following day<br>Became the next day’s master, till the last<br>Made former wonders its. Today the French,<br>All clinquant all in gold, like heathen gods<br>Shone down the English; and tomorrow they<br>Made Britain India. Every man that stood<br>Showed like a mine. Their dwarfish pages were<br>As cherubim, all gilt; the mesdames, too,<br>Not used to toil, did almost sweat to bear<br>The [[concepts/pride\|pride]] upon them, that their very labour<br>Was to them as a painting. Now this [[forms/masque\|masque]]<br>Was cried incomparable, and th‘ensuing [[motifs/night\|night]]<br>Made it a [[archetypes/fool\|fool]] and beggar. The two kings<br>Equal in lustre, were now best, now worst,<br>As presence did present them. Him in eye<br>Still him in praise, and being present both,<br>’Twas said they saw but one, and no discerner<br>Durst wag his tongue in censure. When these suns—<br>For so they phrase ’em—by their heralds challenged<br>The noble spirits to arms, they did perform<br>Beyond thought’s compass, that former fabulous story<br>Being now seen possible enough, got credit<br>That Bevis was believed. |
| [[characters/buckingham\|BUCKINGHAM]] | O, you go far! |
| NORFOLK | As I belong to worship, and affect<br>In [[concepts/honour\|honour]] honesty, the tract of ev’rything<br>Would by a good discourser lose some life<br>Which action’s [[concepts/self\|self]] was tongue to. All was royal.<br>To the disposing of it naught rebelled.<br>Order gave each thing view. The office did<br>Distinctly his full function. |
| [[characters/buckingham\|BUCKINGHAM]] | Who did guide—<br>I mean, who set the [[concepts/body\|body]] and the limbs<br>Of this great sport together, as you guess? |
| NORFOLK | One, certes, that promises no element<br>In such a business. |
| [[characters/buckingham\|BUCKINGHAM]] | I pray you who, my lord? |
| NORFOLK | All this was ordered by the good discretion<br>Of the right reverend Cardinal of York. |
| [[characters/buckingham\|BUCKINGHAM]] | The devil [[characters/speed\|speed]] him! No man’s pie is freed<br>From his ambitious finger. What had he<br>To do in these fierce vanities? I [[concepts/wonder\|wonder]]<br>That such a keech can, with his very bulk,<br>Take up the rays o’th’ beneficial sun,<br>And keep it from the earth. |
| NORFOLK | Surely, sir,<br>There’s in him stuff that puts him to these ends.<br>For being not propped by ancestry, whose grace<br>Chalks successors their way, nor called upon no<br>For high feats done to th’ [[motifs/crown\|crown]], neither allied<br>To eminent assistants, but spider-like,<br>Out of his [[concepts/self\|self]]-drawing web, a gives us note<br>The force of his own merit makes his way—<br>A gift that heaven gives for him which buys<br>A place next to the [[archetypes/king\|King]]. |
| ABERGAVENNY | I cannot tell<br>What heaven hath given him—let some graver eye<br>Pierce into that; but I can see his [[concepts/pride\|pride]]<br>Peep through each part of him. Whence has he that?<br>If not from hell, the devil is a niggard<br>Or has given all before, and he begins<br>A new hell in himself. |
| [[characters/buckingham\|BUCKINGHAM]] | Why the devil,<br>Upon this French going out, took he upon him<br>Without the privity o’th’ [[archetypes/king\|King]] t’appoint<br>Who should attend on him? He makes up the file<br>Of all the gentry, for the most part such<br>To whom as great a charge as little [[concepts/honour\|honour]]<br>He meant to lay upon; and his own letter,<br>The honourable board of council out,<br>Must fetch him in, he papers. |
| ABERGAVENNY | I do know<br>Kinsmen of mine—three at the least—that have<br>By this so sickened their estates that never<br>They shall abound as formerly. |
| [[characters/buckingham\|BUCKINGHAM]] | O, many<br>Have broke their backs with laying manors on ’em<br>For this great [[motifs/journey\|journey]]. What did this [[concepts/vanity\|vanity]]<br>But minister communication of<br>A most poor issue? |
| NORFOLK | Grievingly I think<br>The peace between the French and us not values<br>The cost that did conclude it. |
| [[characters/buckingham\|BUCKINGHAM]] | Every man,<br>After the hideous [[motifs/storm\|storm]] that followed, was<br>A thing inspired, and, not consulting, broke<br>Into a general prophecy—that this tempest,<br>Dashing the garment of this peace, aboded<br>The sudden breach on’t. |
| NORFOLK | Which is budded out—<br>For France hath flawed the league, and hath attached<br>Our merchants’ goods at Bordeaux. |
| ABERGAVENNY | Is it therefore<br>Th’ambassador is silenced? |
| NORFOLK | Marry is’t. |
| ABERGAVENNY | A proper title of a peace, and purchased<br>At a superfluous rate. |
| [[characters/buckingham\|BUCKINGHAM]] | Why, all this business<br>Our reverend Cardinal carried. |
| NORFOLK | Like it your grace,<br>The state takes notice of the private difference<br>Betwixt you and the Cardinal. I advise you—<br>And take it from a heart that wishes towards you<br>[[concepts/honour\|Honour]] and plenteous safety—that you read<br>The Cardinal’s malice and his potency<br>Together; to consider further that<br>What his high hatred would effect wants not<br>A minister in his power. You know his [[concepts/nature\|nature]],<br>That he’s revengeful; and I know his sword<br>Hath a sharp edge—it’s long, and’t may be said no<br>It reaches far; and where ’twill not extend<br>Thither he darts it. Bosom up my counsel,<br>You’ll find it wholesome. Lo, where comes that rock<br>That I advise your shunning. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|Cardinal Wolsey]], the purse containing the great seal borne before him. Enter with him certain of the guard, and two secretaries with papers. The Cardinal in his passage fixeth his eye on [[characters/buckingham\|Buckingham]] and [[characters/buckingham\|Buckingham]] on him, both full of disdain |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | (to a secretary)<br>The [[characters/duke-of-buckingham\|Duke of Buckingham]]’s surveyor, ha?<br>Where’s his examination? |
| SECRETARY | Here, so please you. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | Is he in person ready? |
| SECRETARY | Ay, please your grace. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | [[motifs/well\|Well]], we shall then know more, and [[characters/buckingham\|Buckingham]] Shall lessen this big look. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt Wolsey and his train |
| [[characters/buckingham\|BUCKINGHAM]] | This butcher’s cur is venom-mouthed, and I<br>Have not the power to muzzle him; therefore best<br>Not wake him in his slumber. A beggar’s book<br>Outworths a noble’s [[motifs/blood\|blood]]. |
| NORFOLK | What, are you chafed?<br>Ask God for temp’rance; that’s th’appliance only<br>Which your disease requires. |
| [[characters/buckingham\|BUCKINGHAM]] | I read in’s looks<br>Matter against me, and his eye reviled<br>Me as his abject object. At this instant<br>He bores me with some trick. He’s gone to th’ [[archetypes/king\|King]]—<br>I’ll follow, and outstare him. |
| NORFOLK | Stay, my lord,<br>And let your reason with your choler question<br>What ’tis you go about. To climb steep hills<br>Requires slow pace at first. Anger is like<br>A full hot [[motifs/horse\|horse]] who, being allowed his way,<br>[[concepts/self\|Self]]-mettle tires him. Not a man in England<br>Can advise me like you. Be to yourself<br>As you would to your friend. |
| [[characters/buckingham\|BUCKINGHAM]] | I’ll to the [[archetypes/king\|King]],<br>And from a mouth of [[concepts/honour\|honour]] quite cry down<br>This Ipswich fellow’s insolence, or proclaim<br>There’s difference in no persons. |
| NORFOLK | Be advised.<br>Heat not a furnace for your foe so hot<br>That it do singe yourself. We may outrun<br>By violent swiftness that which we run at,<br>And lose by over-running. Know you not<br>The [[motifs/fire\|fire]] that mounts the liquor till’t run o’er<br>In seeming to augment it wastes it? Be advised.<br>I say again there is no English [[motifs/soul\|soul]]<br>More stronger to direct you than yourself,<br>If with the sap of reason you would quench<br>Or but allay the [[motifs/fire\|fire]] of passion. |
| [[characters/buckingham\|BUCKINGHAM]] | Sir,<br>I am thankful to you, and I’ll go along<br>By your prescription; but this top-proud fellow—<br>Whom from the flow of gall I name not, but<br>From sincere motions—by intelligence,<br>And proofs as clear as founts in July when<br>We see each grain of gravel, I do know<br>To be corrupt and treasonous. |
| NORFOLK | Say not ‘treasonous’. |
| [[characters/buckingham\|BUCKINGHAM]] | To th’ [[archetypes/king\|King]] I’ll say’t, and make my vouch as strong<br>As shore of rock. Attend: this holy fox,<br>Or wolf, or both—for he is equal rav’nous<br>As he is subtle, and as prone to mischief<br>As able to perform’t, his mind and place<br>Infecting one another, yea, reciprocatly—<br>Only to show his pomp as [[motifs/well\|well]] in France<br>As here at [[motifs/home\|home]], suggests the [[archetypes/king\|King]] our master<br>To this last costly treaty, th’interview<br>That swallowed so much treasure and, like a glass,<br>Did break i’th’ rinsing. |
| NORFOLK | [[concepts/faith\|Faith]], and so it did. |
| [[characters/buckingham\|BUCKINGHAM]] | Pray give me favour, sir. This cunning Cardinal,<br>The articles o‘th’ combination drew<br>As himself pleased, and they were ratified 170<br>As he cried ‘Thus let be’, to as much end<br>As give a crutch to th’ dead. But our count-Cardinal<br>Has done this, and ’tis [[motifs/well\|well]] for worthy Wolsey,<br>Who cannot err, he did it. Now this follows—<br>Which, as I take it, is a kind of puppy<br>To th‘old dam, treason—Charles the Emperor,<br>Under pretence to see the [[characters/queen\|Queen]] his aunt—<br>For ’twas indeed his colour, but he came<br>To whisper Wolsey—here makes visitation.<br>His fears were that the interview betwixt<br>England and France might through their amity<br>Breed him some prejudice, for from this league<br>Peeped harms that menaced him. Privily he<br>Deals with our Cardinal and, as I trow—<br>Which I do [[motifs/well\|well]], for I am sure the Emperor<br>Paid ere he promised, whereby his suit was granted<br>Ere it was asked—but when the way was made,<br>And paved with gold, the Emperor thus desired<br>That he would please to alter the [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s course<br>And break the foresaid peace. Let the [[archetypes/king\|King]] know,<br>As soon he shall by me, that thus the Cardinal<br>Does buy and sell his [[concepts/honour\|honour]] as he pleases,<br>And for his own advantage. |
| NORFOLK | I am sorry<br>To hear this of him, and could wish he were<br>Something mistaken in’t. |
| [[characters/buckingham\|BUCKINGHAM]] | No, not a syllable.<br>I do pronounce him in that very shape<br>He shall appear in proof. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Brandon, a serjeant-at-arms before him, and two or three of the guard |
| BRANDON | Your office, serjeant, execute it. |
| SERJEANT | Sir.<br>(To [[characters/buckingham\|Buckingham]]) My lord the [[characters/duke-of-buckingham\|Duke of Buckingham]] and<br>Earl<br>Of Hereford, Stafford, and Northampton, I<br>Arrest thee of high treason in the name<br>Of our most sovereign [[archetypes/king\|King]]. |
| [[characters/buckingham\|BUCKINGHAM]] | to Norfolk<br>Lo you, my lord,<br>The net has fall’n upon me. I shall perish<br>Under device and practice. |
| BRANDON | I am sorry<br>To see you ta’en from liberty to look on<br>The business present. ’Tis his highness’ pleasure<br>You shall to th’ Tower. |
| [[characters/buckingham\|BUCKINGHAM]] | It will help me nothing<br>To plead mine innocence, for that dye is on me<br>Which makes my whit’st part black. The will of<br>heav’n<br>Be done in this and all things. I obey.<br>O, my lord Abergavenny, fare you [[motifs/well\|well]]. |
| BRANDON | Nay, he must bear you company.<br>(To Abergavenny)<br>The [[archetypes/king\|King]]<br>Is pleased you shall to th’ Tower till you know<br>How he determines further. |
| ABERGAVENNY | As the Duke said,<br>The will of heaven be done and the [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s pleasure<br>By me obeyed. |
| BRANDON | Here is a warrant from<br>The [[archetypes/king\|King]] t’attach Lord Montague and the bodies<br>Of the duke’s confessor, John de la Car,<br>One Gilbert Perk, his chancellor— |
| [[characters/buckingham\|BUCKINGHAM]] | So, so;<br>These are the limbs o’th’ plot. No more, I [[concepts/hope\|hope]]. |
| BRANDON | A monk o’th’ Chartreux. |
| [[characters/buckingham\|BUCKINGHAM]] | O, Nicholas Hopkins? |
| BRANDON | He. |
| [[characters/buckingham\|BUCKINGHAM]] | My surveyor is false. The o’er-great Cardinal<br>Hath showed him gold. My life is spanned already.<br>I am the [[archetypes/shadow\|shadow]] of poor [[characters/buckingham\|Buckingham]],<br>Whose figure even this instant cloud puts on<br>By dark’ning my clear sun. (To Norfolk) My lord,<br>farewell. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt Norfolk at one door, [[characters/buckingham\|Buckingham]] and Abergavenny under guard at another |

### Atto 1, Scena 2

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Cornetts. Enter [[archetypes/king\|King]] Henry leaning on [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|Cardinal Wolsey]]’s shoulder. Enter with them Wolsey’s two secretaries, the nobles, and Sir Thomas Lovell. The [[archetypes/king\|King]] ascends to his seat under the cloth of state; Wolsey places himself under the [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s feet on his right side |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | to Wolsey<br>My life itself and the best heart of it<br>Thanks you for this great care. I stood i’th’ level<br>Of a full-charged confederacy, and give thanks<br>To you that choked it. Let be called before us<br>That gentleman of [[characters/buckingham\|Buckingham]]’s. In person<br>I’ll hear him his confessions justify,<br>And point by point the treasons of his master<br>He shall again relate. |
| *(didascalia)* | CRIER (within) |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Room for the [[characters/queen\|Queen]], ushered by the Duke of Norfolk. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/queen-katherine\|Queen Katherine]], the Duke of Norfolk, and the [[characters/duke-of-suffolk\|Duke of Suffolk]]. She kneels. [[archetypes/king\|King]] Henry riseth from his state, takes her up, and kisses her |
| [[characters/queen-katherine\|QUEEN KATHERINE]] | Nay, we must longer kneel. I am a suitor. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Arise, and take place by us.<br>He placeth her by him<br>Half your suit<br>Never name to us. You have half our power,<br>The other moiety ere you ask is given.<br>Repeat your will and take it. |
| [[characters/queen-katherine\|QUEEN KATHERINE]] | Thank your majesty.<br>That you would [[motifs/love\|love]] yourself, and in that [[motifs/love\|love]]<br>Not unconsidered leave your [[concepts/honour\|honour]] nor<br>The dignity of your office, is the point<br>Of my petition. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Lady mine, proceed. |
| [[characters/queen-katherine\|QUEEN KATHERINE]] | I am solicited, not by a few,<br>And those of true condition, that your subjects<br>Are in great grievance. There have been commissions<br>Sent down among ’em which hath flawed the heart<br>Of all their loyalties; wherein, although,<br>My good lord Cardinal, they vent reproaches<br>Most bitterly on you, as putter-on<br>Of these exactions, yet the [[archetypes/king\|King]] our master—<br>Whose [[concepts/honour\|honour]] heaven shield from soil—even he<br>escapes not<br>Language unmannerly, yea, such which breaks<br>The sides of loyalty, and almost appears<br>In loud rebellion. |
| NORFOLK | Not ‘almost appears’—<br>It doth appear; for upon these taxations<br>The clothiers all, not able to maintain<br>The many to them ‘[[concepts/longing\|longing]], have put off<br>The spinsters, carders, fullers, weavers, who,<br>Unfit for other life, compelled by hunger<br>And lack of other means, in desperate manner<br>Daring th’event to th’ teeth, are all in uproar,<br>And danger serves among them. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Taxation?<br>Wherein, and what taxation? My lord Cardinal,<br>You that are blamed for it alike with us,<br>Know you of this taxation? |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | Please you, sir,<br>I know but of a single part in aught<br>Pertains to th’ state, and front but in that file<br>Where others tell steps with me. |
| [[characters/queen-katherine\|QUEEN KATHERINE]] | No, my lord?<br>You know no more than others? But you frame<br>Things that are known alike, which are not wholesome<br>To those which would not know them, and yet must<br>Perforce be their acquaintance. These exactions<br>Whereof my sovereign would have note, they are<br>Most pestilent to th’ hearing, and to bear ’em<br>The back is sacrifice to th’ load. They say<br>They are devised by you, or else you suffer<br>Too hard an exclamation. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Still exaction!<br>The [[concepts/nature\|nature]] of it? In what kind, let’s know,<br>Is this exaction? |
| [[characters/queen-katherine\|QUEEN KATHERINE]] | I am much too venturous<br>In tempting of your patience, but am boldened<br>Under your promised pardon. The subjects’ grief<br>Comes through commissions which compels from each<br>The sixth part of his substance to be levied<br>Without delay, and the pretence for this<br>Is named your wars in France. This makes bold mouths.<br>Tongues spit their duties out, and cold hearts freeze<br>Allegiance in them. Their curses now<br>Live where their prayers did, and it’s come to pass<br>This tractable obedience is a slave<br>To each incensed will. I would your highness<br>Would give it quick consideration, for<br>There is no primer business. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | By my life,<br>This is against our pleasure. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | And for me,<br>I have no further gone in this than by<br>A single voice, and that not passed me but<br>By learned approbation of the judges. If I am<br>Traduced by ignorant tongues, which neither know<br>My faculties nor person yet will be<br>The chronicles of my doing, let me say<br>‘Tis but the fate of place, and the rough brake<br>That virtue must go through. We must not stint<br>Our necessary actions in the [[concepts/fear\|fear]]<br>To cope malicious censurers, which ever,<br>As rav’nous fishes, do a vessel follow<br>That is new trimmed, but benefit no further<br>Than vainly [[concepts/longing\|longing]]. What we oft do best,<br>By sick interpreters, once weak ones, is<br>Not ours or not allowed; what worst, as oft,<br>Hitting a grosser quality, is cried up<br>For our best act. If we shall stand still,<br>In [[concepts/fear\|fear]] our motion will be mocked or carped at,<br>We should take root here where we sit,<br>Or sit state-statues only. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Things done [[motifs/well\|well]],<br>And with a care, exempt themselves from [[concepts/fear\|fear]];<br>Things done without example, in their issue<br>Are to be feared. Have you a precedent<br>Of this commission? I believe not any.<br>We must not rend our subjects from our laws<br>And stick them in our will. Sixth part of each?<br>A trembling contribution! Why, we take<br>From every tree lop, bark, and part o‘th’ timber,<br>And though we leave it with a root, thus hacked<br>The air will drink the sap. To every county<br>Where this is questioned send our letters with<br>Free pardon to each man that has denied<br>The force of this commission. Pray look to’t—<br>I put it to your care. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | (to a secretary) A word with you.<br>Let there be letters writ to every shire<br>Of the [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s grace and pardon.<br>(Aside to the secretary)<br>The grieved commons<br>Hardly conceive of me. Let it be noised<br>That through our intercession this revokement<br>And pardon comes. I shall anon advise you<br>Further in the proceeding. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit secretary |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/buckingham\|Buckingham]]’s Surveyor |
| [[characters/queen-katherine\|QUEEN KATHERINE]] | (to the [[archetypes/king\|King]])<br>I am sorry that the [[characters/duke-of-buckingham\|Duke of Buckingham]]<br>Is run in your displeasure. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | It grieves many.<br>The gentleman is learned, and a most rare speaker,<br>To [[concepts/nature\|nature]] none more bound; his training such<br>That he may furnish and instruct great teachers<br>And never seek for aid out of himself. Yet see,<br>When these so noble benefits shall prove<br>Not [[motifs/well\|well]] disposed, the mind growing once corrupt,<br>They turn to vicious forms ten times more ugly<br>Than ever they were fair. This man so complete,<br>Who was enrolled ’mongst wonders—and when we<br>Almost with ravished list’ning could not find<br>His hour of speech a minute—he, my lady,<br>Hath into monstrous habits put the graces<br>That once were his, and is become as black<br>As if besmeared in hell. Sit by us. You shall hear—<br>This was his gentleman in trust of him—<br>Things to strike [[concepts/honour\|honour]] sad.<br>(To Wolsey)<br>Bid him recount<br>The fore-recited practices whereof<br>We cannot feel too little, hear too much. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | (to the Surveyor)<br>Stand forth, and with bold spirit relate what you<br>Most like a careful subject have collected<br>Out of the [[characters/duke-of-buckingham\|Duke of Buckingham]]. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | (to the Surveyor)<br>Speak freely. |
| [[characters/buckingham\|BUCKINGHAM]]’S SURVEYOR | First, it was usual with him, every day<br>It would infect his speech, that if the [[archetypes/king\|King]]<br>Should without issue die, he’ll carry it so<br>To make the sceptre his. These very words<br>I’ve heard him utter to his son-in-law,<br>Lord Abergavenny, to whom by oath he menaced<br>[[concepts/revenge\|Revenge]] upon the Cardinal. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | (to the [[archetypes/king\|King]])<br>Please your highness note<br>His dangerous conception in this point,<br>Not friended by his wish to your high person.<br>His will is most malignant, and it stretches<br>Beyond you to your friends. |
| [[characters/queen-katherine\|QUEEN KATHERINE]] | My learned Lord Cardinal,<br>Deliver all with [[concepts/charity\|charity]]. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | (to the surveyor) Speak on.<br>How grounded he his title to the [[motifs/crown\|crown]]<br>Upon our fail? To this point hast thou heard him<br>At any [[motifs/time\|time]] speak aught? |
| [[characters/buckingham\|BUCKINGHAM]]’S SURVEYOR | He was brought to this<br>By a vain prophecy of Nicholas Hopkins. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | What was that Hopkins? |
| [[characters/buckingham\|BUCKINGHAM]]’S SURVEYOR | Sir, a Chartreux friar,<br>His confessor, who fed him every minute<br>With words of sovereignty. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | How know’st thou this? |
| [[characters/buckingham\|BUCKINGHAM]]’S SURVEYOR | Not long before your highness sped to France,<br>The Duke being at the [[motifs/rose\|Rose]], within the parish<br>Saint Lawrence Poutney, did of me demand<br>What was the speech among the Londoners<br>Concerning the French [[motifs/journey\|journey]]. I replied<br>Men feared the French would prove perfidious,<br>To the [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s danger; presently the Duke<br>Said ‘twas the [[concepts/fear\|fear]] indeed, and that he doubted<br>’Twould prove the verity of certain words<br>Spoke by a holy monk that oft, says he,<br>‘Hath sent to me, wishing me to permit<br>John de la Car, my chaplain, a choice hour<br>To hear from him a matter of some moment;<br>Whom after under the confession’s seal<br>He solemnly had sworn, that what he spoke<br>My chaplain to no creature living but<br>To me should utter, with demure confidence<br>This pausingly ensued: “neither the [[archetypes/king\|King]] nor’s heirs”,<br>Tell you the Duke, “shall prosper. Bid him strive<br>To win the [[motifs/love\|love]] o’th’ commonalty. The Duke<br>Shall govern England.” ’ |
| [[characters/queen-katherine\|QUEEN KATHERINE]] | If I know you [[motifs/well\|well]],<br>You were the Duke’s surveyor, and lost your office<br>On the complaint o’th’ tenants. Take good heed<br>You charge not in your spleen a noble person<br>And spoil your nobler [[motifs/soul\|soul]]. I say, take heed;<br>Yes, heartily beseech you. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Let him on.<br>(To the Surveyor) Go forward. |
| [[characters/buckingham\|BUCKINGHAM]]’S SURVEYOR | On my [[motifs/soul\|soul]] I’ll speak but [[concepts/truth\|truth]].<br>I told my lord the Duke, by th’ devil’s illusions<br>The monk might be deceived, and that ‘twas<br>dangerous<br>To ruminate on this so far until<br>It forged him some design which, being believed,<br>It was much like to do. He answered, ’Tush,<br>It can do me no damage’, adding further<br>That had the [[archetypes/king\|King]] in his last sickness failed,<br>The Cardinal’s and Sir Thomas Lovell’s heads<br>Should have gone off. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Ha? What, so rank? Ah, ha!<br>There’s mischief in this man. Canst thou say further? |
| [[characters/buckingham\|BUCKINGHAM]]’S SURVEYOR | I can, my liege. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Proceed. |
| BIJCKINGHAM’S SURVEYOR | Being at Greenwich,<br>After your highness had reproved the Duke<br>About Sir William Bulmer— |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | I remember<br>Such a [[motifs/time\|time]], being my sworn servant,<br>The Duke retained him his. But on—what hence? |
| RIICKINGHAM’S SURVEYOR | ‘If’, quoth he, ‘I for this had been committed’—<br>As to the Tower, I thought—‘I would have played<br>The part my father meant to act upon<br>Th’[[archetypes/usurper\|usurper]] [[characters/richard\|Richard]] who, being at Salisbury,<br>Made suit to come in’s presence; which if granted,<br>As he made semblance of his duty, would<br>Have put his knife into him.’ |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | A giant traitor! |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | (to the [[characters/queen\|Queen]])<br>Now, madam, may his highness live in [[concepts/freedom\|freedom]],<br>And this man out of [[motifs/prison\|prison]]? |
| [[characters/queen-katherine\|QUEEN KATHERINE]] | God mend all. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | (to the Surveyor)<br>There’s something more would out of thee—what<br>sayst? |
| [[characters/buckingham\|BUCKINGHAM]]’S SURVEYOR | After ‘the Duke his father’, with ‘the knife’,<br>He stretched him, and with one hand on his dagger,<br>Another spread on’s breast, mounting his eyes,<br>He did discharge a horrible oath whose tenor<br>Was, were he evil used, he would outgo<br>His father by as much as a performance<br>Does an irresolute purpose. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | There’s his period—<br>To sheathe his knife in us. He is attached.<br>Call him to present trial. If he may<br>Find [[concepts/mercy\|mercy]] in the law, ’tis his; if none,<br>Let him not seek’t of us. By day and [[motifs/night\|night]],<br>He’s traitor to th’ height. |
| *(didascalia)* | Flourish. Exeunt |

### Atto 1, Scena 3

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Lord Chamberlain and Lord Sands |
| LORD CHAMBERLAIN | Is’t possible the spells of France should juggle<br>Men into such strange mysteries? |
| SANDS | New customs,<br>Though they be never so ridiculous—<br>Nay, let ’em be unmanly—yet are followed. |
| LORD CHAMBERLAIN | As far as I see, all the good our English<br>Have got by the late voyage is but merely<br>A fit or two o’th’ face. But they are shrewd ones,<br>For when they hold ’em you would swear directly<br>Their very noses had been counsellors<br>To Pepin or Clotharius, they keep state so. |
| SANDS | They have all new legs, and lame ones; one would<br>take it,<br>That never see ’em pace before, the spavin<br>Or [[motifs/spring\|spring]]-halt reigned among ’em. |
| LORD CHAMBERLAIN | [[motifs/death\|Death]], my lord,<br>Their clothes are after such a pagan cut to’t<br>That sure they’ve worn out Christendom. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Sir Thomas Lovell |
| LORD CHAMBERLAIN | How now—<br>What news, Sir Thomas Lovell? |
| LOVELL | [[concepts/faith\|Faith]], my lord,<br>I hear of none but the new proclamation<br>That’s clapped upon the [[settings/court\|court]] gate. |
| LORD CHAMBERLAIN | What is’t for? |
| LOVELL | The [[historical-references/reformation\|reformation]] of our travelled gallants<br>That fill the [[settings/court\|court]] with quarrels, talk, and tailors. |
| LORD CHAMBERLAIN | I’m glad ’tis there. Now I would pray our ’messieurs’<br><br>To think an English courtier may be wise<br>And never see the Louvre. |
| LOVELL | They must either,<br>For so run the conditions, leave those remnants<br>Of [[archetypes/fool\|fool]] and feather that they got in France,<br>With all their honourable points of ignorance<br>Pertaining thereunto—as fights and fireworks,<br>Abusing better men than they can be<br>Out of a foreign wisdom, renouncing clean<br>The [[concepts/faith\|faith]] they have in tennis and tall stockings,<br>Short blistered breeches, and those types of travel—<br>And understand again like honest men,<br>Or pack to their old playfellows. There, I take it,<br>They may, cum privilegio, ‘oui’ away<br>The lag end of their lewdness and be laughed at. |
| SANDS | ‘Tis [[motifs/time\|time]] to give ’em physic, their diseases<br>Are grown so catching. |
| LORD CHAMBERLAIN | What a loss our ladies<br>Will have of these trim vanities! |
| LOVELL | Ay, marry,<br>There will be woe indeed, lords. The sly whoresons<br>Have got a speeding trick to lay down ladies.<br>A French song and a fiddle has no fellow. |
| SANDS | The devil fiddle ’em! I am glad they are going,<br>For sure there’s no converting of ’em. Now<br>An honest country lord, as I am, beaten<br>A long [[motifs/time\|time]] out of play, may bring his plainsong<br>And have an hour of hearing, and, by’r Lady,<br>Held current music, too. |
| LORD CHAMBERLAIN | [[motifs/well\|Well]] said, Lord Sands.<br>Your colt’s tooth is not cast yet? |
| SANDS | No, my lord,<br>Nor shall not while I have a stump. |
| LORD CHAMBERLAIN | (to Lovell)<br>Sir Thomas,<br>Whither were you a-going? |
| LOVELL | To the Cardinal’s.<br>Your lordship is a guest too. |
| LORD CHAMBERLAIN | O, ’tis true.<br>This [[motifs/night\|night]] he makes a supper, and a great one,<br>To many lords and ladies. There will be<br>The [[concepts/beauty\|beauty]] of this kingdom, I’ll assure you. |
| LOVELL | That churchman bears a bounteous mind indeed,<br>A hand as fruitful as the land that feeds us.<br>His dews fall everywhere. |
| LORD CHAMBERLAIN | No [[concepts/doubt\|doubt]] he’s noble.<br>He had a black mouth that said other of him. |
| SANDS | He may, my lord; he’s wherewithal. In him<br>Sparing would show a worse sin than ill doctrine.<br>Men of his way should be most liberal.<br>They are set here for examples. |
| LORD CHAMBERLAIN | True, they are so,<br>But few now give so great ones. My barge stays.<br>Your lordship shall along. (To Lovell) Come, good Sir<br>Thomas,<br>We shall be late else, which I would not be,<br>For I was spoke to, with Sir Henry Guildford,<br>This [[motifs/night\|night]] to be comptrollers. |
| SANDS | I am your lordship’s. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |

### Atto 1, Scena 4

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Hautboys. Enter servants with a small table for [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|Cardinal Wolsey]] Which they place under the cloth of state, and a longer table for the guests. Then enter at one door Anne Boleyn and divers other ladies and gentlemen as guests, and at another door enter Sir Henry Guildford |
| *(didascalia)* | GUlLDFORD<br>Ladies, a general welcome from his grace<br>Salutes ye all. This [[motifs/night\|night]] he dedicates<br>To fair content and you. None here, he hopes,<br>In all this noble bevy, has brought with her<br>One care abroad. He would have all as merry<br>As feast, good company, good wine, good welcome<br>Can make good people. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Lord Chamberlain, Lord Sands, and Sir Thomas Lovell |
| *(didascalia)* | (To the Lord Chamberlain) O, my lord, you’re tardy.<br>The very thought of this fair company<br>Clapped wings to me. |
| LORD CHAMBERLAIN | You are young, Sir Harry Guildford. |
| SANDS | Sir Thomas Lovell, had the Cardinal<br>But half my lay thoughts in him, some of these<br>Should find a running banquet, ere they rested,<br>I think would better please ’em. By my life,<br>They are a sweet society of fair ones. |
| LOVELL | O, that your lordship were but now confessor<br>To one or two of these. |
| SANDS | I would I were.<br>They should find easy penance. |
| LOVELL | [[concepts/faith\|Faith]], how easy? |
| SANDS | As easy as a down bed would afford it. |
| LORD CHAMBERLAIN | Sweet ladies, will it please you sit?<br>(To Guildford)<br>Sir Harry,<br>Place you that side, I’ll take the charge of this.<br>They sit about the longer table. A noise within<br>His grace is ent‘ring. Nay, you must not freeze—<br>Two women placed together makes cold weather.<br>My lord Sands, you are one will keep ’em waking.<br>Pray sit between these ladies. |
| SANDS | By my [[concepts/faith\|faith]],<br>And thank your lordship.<br>He sits between Anne and another<br>By your leave, sweet ladies.<br>If I chance to talk a little wild, forgive me.<br>I had it from my father. |
| ANNE | Was he mad, sir? |
| SANDS | O, very mad; exceeding mad—in [[motifs/love\|love]], too.<br>But he would bite none. Just as I do now,<br>He would kiss you twenty with a breath.<br>He kisses her |
| LORD CHAMBERLAIN | [[motifs/well\|Well]] said, my lord.<br>So now you’re fairly seated. Gentlemen,<br>The penance lies on you if these fair ladies<br>Pass away frowning. |
| SANDS | For my little cure,<br>Let me alone. |
| *(didascalia)* | Hautboys. Enter [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|Cardinal Wolsey]] who takes his seat at the small table under the state |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | You’re welcome, my fair guests. That noble lady<br>Or gentleman that is not freely merry<br>Is not my friend. This, to confirm my welcome,<br>And to you all, good health!<br>He drinks |
| SANDS | Your grace is noble.<br>Let me have such a bowl may hold my thanks,<br>And save me so much talking. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | My lord Sands,<br>I am beholden to you. Cheer your neighbours.<br>Ladies, you are not merry! Gentlemen,<br>Whose fault is this? |
| SANDS | The red wine first must rise<br>In their fair cheeks, my lord, then we shall have ’em<br>Talk us to [[motifs/silence\|silence]]. |
| ANNE | You are a merry gamester,<br>My lord Sands. |
| SANDS | Yes, if I make my play.<br>Here’s to your ladyship; and pledge it, madam,<br>For ’tis to such a thing— |
| ANNE | You cannot show me. |
| SANDS | (to Wolsey)<br>I told your grace they would talk anon. |
| *(didascalia)* | Drum and trumpet. Chambers discharged |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | What’s that? |
| LORD CHAMBERLAIN | (to the servants)<br>Look out there, some of ye. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit a servant |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | What warlike voice,<br>And to what end is this? Nay, ladies, [[concepts/fear\|fear]] not.<br>By all the laws of war you’re privileged. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the servant |
| LORD CHAMBERLAIN | How now—what is’t? |
| SERVANT | A noble troop of strangers,<br>For so they seem. They’ve left their barge and landed,<br>And hither make as great ambassadors<br>From foreign princes. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | Good Lord Chamberlain,<br>Go give ’em welcome—you can speak the French<br>tongue.<br>And pray receive ’em nobly, and conduct ’em<br>Into our presence where this heaven of [[concepts/beauty\|beauty]]<br>Shall shine at full upon them. Some attend him. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Chamberlain, attended |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | All rise, and some servants remove the tables<br>You have now a broken banquet, but we’ll mend it.<br>A good digestion to you all, and once more<br>I shower a welcome on ye—welcome all. |
| *(didascalia)* | Hautboys. Enter, ushered by the Lord Chamberlain, [[archetypes/king\|King]] Henry and others as masquers habited like shepherds. They pass directly before [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|Cardinal Wolsey]] and gracefully salute him |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | A noble company. What are their pleasures? |
| LORD CHAMBERLAIN | Because they speak no English, thus they prayed<br>To tell your grace, that, having heard by fame<br>Of this so noble and so fair assembly<br>This [[motifs/night\|night]] to meet here, they could do no less,<br>Out of the great respect they bear to [[concepts/beauty\|beauty]],<br>But leave their flocks, and, under your fair conduct,<br>Crave leave to view these ladies, and entreat<br>An hour of revels with ’em. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | Say, Lord Chamberlain,<br>They have done my poor house grace, for which I pay<br>’em<br>A thousand thanks, and pray ’em take their pleasures.<br>The masquers choose ladies. The [[archetypes/king\|King]] chooses Anne Boleyn |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | (to Anne)<br>The fairest hand I ever touched. O [[concepts/beauty\|beauty]],<br>Till now I never knew thee. |
| *(didascalia)* | Music. They [[motifs/dance\|dance]] |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | (to the Lord Chamberlain) My lord. |
| LORD CHAMBERLAIN | Your grace. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | Pray tell ’em thus much from me. There should be one amongst ’em by his person More worthy this place than myself, to whom, If I but knew him, with my [[motifs/love\|love]] and duty I would surrender it. |
| LORD CHAMBERLAIN | I will, my lord.<br>He whispers with the masquers |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | What say they? |
| LORD CHAMBERLAIN | Such a one they all confess<br>There is indeed, which they would have your grace<br>Find out, and he will take it. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | standing<br>Let me see then.<br>By all your good [[motifs/leaves\|leaves]], gentlemen, here I’ll make<br>My royal choice.<br>He bows before the [[archetypes/king\|King]] |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | unmasking Ye have found him, Cardinal.<br>You hold a fair assembly. You do [[motifs/well\|well]], lord.<br>You are a churchman, or I’ll tell you, Cardinal,<br>I should judge now unhappily. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | I am glad<br>Your grace is grown so pleasant. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | My Lord Chamberlain,<br>Prithee come hither.<br>(Gesturing towards Anne) What fair lady’s that? |
| LORD CHAMBERLAIN | An’t please your grace, Sir Thomas Boleyn’s daughter—<br>The Viscount Rochford—one of her highness’ women. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | By heaven, she is a dainty one. (To Anne) Sweetheart,<br>I were unmannerly to take you out |
| *(didascalia)* | And not to kiss you kisses her. A health, gentlemen; |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | He drinks<br>Let it go round. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | Sir Thomas Lovell, is the banquet ready<br>I’th’ privy chamber? |
| LOVELL | Yes, my lord. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | (to the [[archetypes/king\|King]])<br>Your grace<br>I [[concepts/fear\|fear]] with dancing is a little heated. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | I [[concepts/fear\|fear]] too much. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | There’s fresher air, my lord,<br>In the next chamber. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Lead in your ladies, every one. (To Anne) Sweet partner,<br>I must not yet forsake you. (To Wolsey) Let’s be merry,<br>Good my lord Cardinal. I have half a dozen healths<br>To drink to these fair ladies, and a measure<br>To lead ’em once again, and then let’s dream<br>Who’s best in favour. Let the music knock it. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt with trumpets |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | 2.1 Enter two Gentlemen, at several doors |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | Whither away so fast? |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | O, God save ye.<br>Ev’n to the hall to hear what shall become<br>Of the great [[characters/duke-of-buckingham\|Duke of Buckingham]]. |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | I’ll save you<br>That labour, sir. All’s now done but the ceremony<br>Of bringing back the prisoner. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | Were you there? |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | Yes, indeed was I. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | Pray speak what has happened. |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | You may guess quickly what. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | Is he found guilty? |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | Yes, truly is he, and condemned upon’t. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | I am sorry for’t. |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | So are a number more. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | But pray, how passed it? |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | I’ll tell you in a little. The great Duke<br>Came to the bar, where to his accusations<br>He pleaded still not guilty, and allegèd<br>Many sharp reasons to defeat the law.<br>The [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s attorney, on the contrary,<br>Urged on the examinations, proofs, confessions,<br>Of divers witnesses, which the Duke desired<br>To him brought viva voce to his face—<br>At which appeared against him his surveyor,<br>Sir Gilbert Perk his chancellor, and John Car,<br>Confessor to him, with that devil-monk,<br>Hopkins, that made this mischief. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | That was he<br>That fed him with his prophecies. |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | The same.<br>All these accused him strongly, which he fain<br>Would have flung from him, but indeed he could not.<br>And so his peers, upon this evidence,<br>Have found him guilty of high treason. Much<br>He spoke, and learnedly, for life, but all<br>Was either pitied in him or forgotten. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | After all this, how did he bear himself? |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | When he was brought again to th’ bar to hear<br>His knell rung out, his judgement, he was stirred<br>With such an agony he sweat extremely,<br>And something spoke in choler, ill and hasty;<br>But he fell to himself again, and sweetly<br>In all the rest showed a most noble patience. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | I do not think he fears [[motifs/death\|death]]. |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | Sure he does not.<br>He never was so womanish. The cause<br>He may a little grieve at. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | Certainly<br>The Cardinal is the end of this. |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | ’Tis likely<br>By all conjectures: first, Kildare’s attainder,<br>Then deputy of Ireland, who, removed,<br>Earl Surrey was sent thither—and in haste, too,<br>Lest he should help his father. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | That trick of state<br>Was a deep envious one. |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | At his return<br>No [[concepts/doubt\|doubt]] he will requite it. This is noted,<br>And generally: whoever the [[archetypes/king\|King]] favours,<br>The Card’nal instantly will find employment—<br>And far enough from [[settings/court\|court]], too. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | All the commons<br>Hate him perniciously and, o’ my [[concepts/conscience\|conscience]],<br>Wish him ten fathom deep. This Duke as much<br>They [[motifs/love\|love]] and dote on, call him ‘bounteous<br>[[characters/buckingham\|Buckingham]],<br>The [[motifs/mirror\|mirror]] of all courtesy’— |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the [[characters/duke-of-buckingham\|Duke of Buckingham]] from his arraignment, tipstaves before him, the axe with the edge towards him, halberdiers on each side, accompanied with Sir Thomas Lovell, Sir Nicholas Vaux, Sir William Sands, and common people |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | Stay there, sir,<br>And see the noble ruined man you speak of. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | Let’s stand close and behold him.<br>They stand apart |
| [[characters/buckingham\|BUCKINGHAM]] | (to the common people) All good people,<br>You that thus far have come to pity me,<br>Hear what I say, and then go [[motifs/home\|home]] and lose me.<br>I have this day received a traitor’s judgement,<br>And by that name must die. Yet, heaven bear witness,<br>And if I have a [[concepts/conscience\|conscience]] let it sink me,<br>Even as the axe falls, if I be not faithful.<br>The law I bear no malice for my [[motifs/death\|death]].<br>’T has done, upon the premises, but justice.<br>But those that sought it I could wish more Christians.<br>Be what they will, I heartily forgive ‘em.<br>Yet let ’em look they glory not in mischief,<br>Nor build their evils on the graves of great men,<br>For then my guiltless [[motifs/blood\|blood]] must cry against ‘em.<br>For further life in this world I ne’er [[concepts/hope\|hope]],<br>Nor will I sue, although the [[archetypes/king\|King]] have mercies<br>More than I dare make faults. You few that loved me,<br>And dare be bold to weep for [[characters/buckingham\|Buckingham]],<br>His noble friends and fellows, whom to leave<br>Is only bitter to him, only dying,<br>Go with me like good angels to my end,<br>And, as the long divorce of steel falls on me,<br>Make of your prayers one sweet sacrifice,<br>And lift my [[motifs/soul\|soul]] to heaven. (To the guard) Lead on, i’<br>God’s name. |
| LOVELL | I do beseech your grace, for [[concepts/charity\|charity]],<br>If ever any malice in your heart<br>Were hid against me, now to forgive me frankly. |
| [[characters/buckingham\|BUCKINGHAM]] | Sir Thomas Lovell, I as free forgive you<br>As I would be forgiven. I forgive all.<br>There cannot be those numberless offences<br>‘Gainst me that I cannot take peace with. No black envy<br>Shall mark my [[motifs/grave\|grave]]. Commend me to his grace,<br>And if he speak of [[characters/buckingham\|Buckingham]], pray tell him<br>You met him half in heaven. My vows and prayers<br>Yet are the [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s, and, till my [[motifs/soul\|soul]] forsake,<br>Shall cry for blessings on him. May he live<br>Longer than I have [[motifs/time\|time]] to tell his years;<br>Ever beloved and loving may his rule be;<br>And, when old [[motifs/time\|time]] shall lead him to his end,<br>Goodness and he fill up one monument. |
| LOVELL | To th’ waterside I must conduct your grace,<br>Then give my charge up to Sir Nicholas Vaux,<br>Who undertakes you to your end. |
| VAUX | (to an attendant) Prepare there—<br>The Duke is coming. See the barge be ready,<br>And fit it with such furniture as suits<br>The [[concepts/greatness\|greatness]] of his person. |
| [[characters/buckingham\|BUCKINGHAM]] | Nay, Sir Nicholas,<br>Let it alone. My state now will but mock me.<br>When I came hither I was Lord High Constable<br>And [[characters/duke-of-buckingham\|Duke of Buckingham]]; now, poor Edward Bohun.<br>Yet I am richer than my base accusers,<br>That never knew what [[concepts/truth\|truth]] meant. I now seal it,<br>And with that [[motifs/blood\|blood]] will make ’em one day groan for’t.<br>My noble father, Henry of [[characters/buckingham\|Buckingham]],<br>Who first raised head against usurping [[characters/richard\|Richard]],<br>Flying for succour to his servant Banister,<br>Being distressed, was by that wretch betrayed,<br>And without trial fell. God’s peace be with him.<br>Henry the Seventh succeeding, truly pitying<br>My father’s loss, like a most royal prince,<br>Restored me to my honours, and out of ruins<br>Made my name once more noble. Now his son,<br>Henry the Eighth, life, [[concepts/honour\|honour]], name, and all<br>That made me happy, at one stroke has taken<br>For ever from the world. I had my trial,<br>And must needs say a noble one; which makes me<br>A little happier than my wretched father.<br>Yet thus far we are one in fortunes: both<br>Fell by our servants, by those men we loved most—<br>A most unnatural and faithless service.<br>Heaven has an end in all. Yet, you that hear me,<br>This from a dying man receive as certain—<br>Where you are liberal of your loves and counsels,<br>Be sure you be not loose; for those you make friends<br>And give your hearts to, when they once perceive<br>The least rub in your fortunes, fall away<br>Like [[motifs/water\|water]] from ye, never found again<br>But where they mean to sink ye. All good people<br>Pray for me. I must now forsake ye. The last hour<br>Of my long weary life is come upon me.<br>Farewell, and when you would say something that is<br>sad,<br>Speak how I fell. I have done, and God forgive me. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt [[characters/buckingham\|Buckingham]] and train |
| [[characters/buckingham\|BUCKINGHAM]] | The two Gentlemen come forward |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | O, this is full of pity, sir; it calls,<br>I [[concepts/fear\|fear]], too many curses on their heads<br>That were the authors. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | If the Duke be guiltless,<br>’Tis full of woe. Yet I can give you inkling<br>Of an ensuing evil, if it fall,<br>Greater than this. |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | Good angels keep it from us.<br>What may it be? You do not [[concepts/doubt\|doubt]] my [[concepts/faith\|faith]], sir? |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | This secret is so weighty, ’twill require<br>A strong [[concepts/faith\|faith]] to conceal it. |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | Let me have it—<br>I do not talk much. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | I am confident;<br>You shall, sir. Did you not of late days hear<br>A buzzing of separation<br>Between the [[archetypes/king\|King]] and Katherine? |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | Yes, but it held not.<br>For when the [[archetypes/king\|King]] once heard it, out of anger<br>He sent command to the [[characters/lord-mayor\|Lord Mayor]] straight<br>To stop the rumour and allay those tongues<br>That durst disperse it. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | But that slander, sir,<br>Is found a [[concepts/truth\|truth]] now, for it grows again<br>Fresher than e’er it was, and held for certain<br>The [[archetypes/king\|King]] will venture at it. Either the Cardinal<br>Or some about him near have, out of malice<br>To the good [[characters/queen\|Queen]], possessed him with a scruple<br>That will undo her. To confirm this, too,<br>Cardinal Campeius is arrived, and lately,<br>As all think, for this business. |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | ’Tis the Cardinal;<br>And merely to [[concepts/revenge\|revenge]] him on the Emperor<br>For not bestowing on him at his asking<br>The Archbishopric of Toledo this is purposed. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | I think you have hit the mark. But is’t not cruel<br>That she should feel the smart of this? The Cardinal<br>Will have his will, and she must fall. |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | ’Tis woeful.<br>We are too open here to argue this.<br>Let’s think in private more. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |

### Atto 2, Scena 2

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Lord Chamberlain with a letter |
| LORD CHAMBERLAIN | (reads) ‘My lord, the horses your lordship sent for, with all the care I had, I saw [[motifs/well\|well]] chosen, ridden, and furnished. They were young and handsome, and of the best breed in the north. When they were ready to set out for [[settings/london\|London]], a man of my lord Cardinal’s, by commission and main power, took ‘em from me with this reason—his master would be served before a subject, if not before the [[archetypes/king\|King]]; which stopped our mouths, sir.’<br>I [[concepts/fear\|fear]] he will indeed. [[motifs/well\|Well]], let him have them. 10<br>He will have all, I think. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter to the Lord Chamberlain the Dukes of Norfolk and Suffolk |
| NORFOLK | [[motifs/well\|Well]] met, my Lord Chamberlain. |
| LORD CHAMBERLAIN | Good day to both your graces. |
| SUFFOLK | How is the [[archetypes/king\|King]] employed? |
| LORD CHAMBERLAIN | I left him private,<br>Full of sad thoughts and troubles. |
| NORFOLK | What’s the cause? |
| LORD CHAMBERLAIN | It seems the [[motifs/marriage\|marriage]] with his brother’s wife<br>Has crept too near his [[concepts/conscience\|conscience]]. |
| SUFFOLK | No, his [[concepts/conscience\|conscience]]<br>Has crept too near another lady. |
| NORFOLK | ’Tis so.<br>This is the Cardinal’s doing. The [[archetypes/king\|King]]-Cardinal,<br>That blind priest, like the eldest son of fortune,<br>Turns what he list. The [[archetypes/king\|King]] will know him one day. |
| SUFFOLK | Pray God he do. He’ll never know himself else. |
| NORFOLK | How holily he works in all his business,<br>And with what zeal! For now he has cracked the<br>league<br>Between us and the Emperor, the [[characters/queen\|Queen]]’s greatnephew,<br>25<br>He dives into the [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s [[motifs/soul\|soul]] and there scatters<br>Dangers, doubts, wringing of the [[concepts/conscience\|conscience]],<br>Fears, and despairs—and all these for his [[motifs/marriage\|marriage]].<br>And out of all these, to restore the [[archetypes/king\|King]],<br>He counsels a divorce—a loss of her<br>That like a [[motifs/jewel\|jewel]] has hung twenty years<br>About his neck, yet never lost her lustre;<br>Of her that loves him with that excellence<br>That angels [[motifs/love\|love]] good men with; even of her<br>That, when the greatest stroke of fortune falls,<br>Will bless the [[archetypes/king\|King]]—and is not this course pious? |
| LORD CHAMBERLAIN | Heaven keep me from such counsel! ’Tis most true—<br>These news are everywhere, every tongue speaks ’em,<br>And every true heart weeps for’t. All that dare<br>Look into these affairs see this main end—<br>The French [[archetypes/king\|king]]’s sister. Heaven will one day open<br>The [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s eyes, that so long have slept, upon<br>This bold bad man. |
| SUFFOLK | And free us from his [[concepts/slavery\|slavery]]. |
| NORFOLK. | We had need pray,<br>And heartily, for our deliverance,<br>Or this imperious man will work us all<br>From princes into pages. All men’s honours<br>Lie like one lump before him, to be fashioned<br>Into what pitch he please. |
| SUFFOLK | For me, my lords,<br>I [[motifs/love\|love]] him not, nor [[concepts/fear\|fear]] him—there’s my creed.<br>As I am made without him, so I’ll stand,<br>If the [[archetypes/king\|King]] please. His curses and his blessings<br>Touch me alike; they’re breath I not believe in.<br>I knew him, and I know him; so I leave him<br>To him that made him proud—the Pope. |
| NORFOLK | Let’s in,<br>And with some other business put the [[archetypes/king\|King]]<br>From these sad thoughts that work too much upon him.<br>(To the Lord Chamberlain)<br>My lord, you’ll bear us company? |
| LORD CHAMBERLAIN | Excuse me,<br>The [[archetypes/king\|King]] has sent me otherwhere. Besides,<br>You’ll find a most unfit [[motifs/time\|time]] to disturb him.<br>Health to your lordships. |
| NORFOLK | Thanks, my good Lord Chamberlain. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit the Lord Chamberlain |
| NORFOLK | [[archetypes/king\|King]] Henry draws the curtain, and sits reading<br>pensively |
| SUFFOLK | How sad he looks! Sure he is much afflicted. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Who’s there? Ha? |
| NORFOLK | Pray God he be not angry. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Who’s there, I say? How dare you thrust yourselves<br>Into my private meditations!<br>Who am I? Ha? |
| NORFOLK | A gracious [[archetypes/king\|king]] that pardons all offences<br>Malice ne’er meant. Our breach of duty this way<br>Is business of estate, in which we come<br>To know your royal pleasure. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Ye are too bold.<br>Go to, I’ll make ye know your times of business.<br>Is this an hour for temporal affairs? Ha? |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|Cardinal Wolsey]] and Cardinal Campeius, the latter with a commission |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Who’s there? My good lord Cardinal? O, my Wolsey,<br>The quiet of my wounded [[concepts/conscience\|conscience]],<br>Thou art a cure fit for a [[archetypes/king\|king]].<br>(To Campeius)<br>You’re welcome,<br>Most learnèd reverend sir, into our kingdom.<br>Use us, and it. (To Wolsey) My good lord, have great<br>care<br>I be not found a talker. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | Sir, you cannot.<br>I would your grace would give us but an hour<br>Of private conference. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | (to Norfolk and Suffolk) We are busy; go.<br>Norfolk and Suffolk speak privately to one another as they depart |
| NORFOLK | This priest has no [[concepts/pride\|pride]] in him! |
| SUFFOLK | Not to speak of.<br>I would not be so sick, though, for his place—<br>But this cannot continue. |
| NORFOLK | If it do<br>I’ll venture one have-at-him. |
| SUFFOLK | I another. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt Norfolk and Suffolk |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | (to the [[archetypes/king\|King]])<br>Your grace has given a precedent of wisdom<br>Above all princes in committing freely<br>Your scruple to the voice of Christendom.<br>Who can be angry now? What envy reach you?<br>The Spaniard, tied by [[motifs/blood\|blood]] and favour to her,<br>Must now confess, if they have any goodness,<br>The trial just and noble. All the cterks—<br>I mean the learnèd ones in Christian kingdoms—<br>Have their free voices. Rome, the [[characters/nurse\|nurse]] of judgement,<br>Invited by your noble [[concepts/self\|self]], hath sent<br>One general tongue unto us: this good man,<br>This just and learned priest, Card’nal Campeius,<br>Whom once more I present unto your highness. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | (embracing Campeius)<br>And once more in mine arms I bid him welcome,<br>And thank the holy conclave for their loves.<br>They have sent me such a man I would have wished for. |
| CARDINAL CAMPEIUS | Your grace must needs deserve all strangers’ loves,<br>You are so noble. To your highness’ hand<br>I tender my commission,<br>He gives the commission to the [[archetypes/king\|King]]<br>(To Wolsey)<br>by whose virtue,<br>The [[settings/court\|Court]] of Rome commanding, you, my lord<br>Cardinal of York, are joined with me their servant<br>In the unpartial judging of this business. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Two equal men. The [[characters/queen\|Queen]] shall be acquainted<br>Forthwith for what you come. Where’s Gardiner? |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | I know your majesty has always loved her<br>So dear in heart not to deny her that<br>A woman of less place might ask by law—<br>Scholars allowed freely to argue for her. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Ay, and the best she shall have, and my favour<br>To him that does best, God forbid else. Cardinal,<br>Prithee call Gardiner to me, my new secretary.<br>[[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|Cardinal Wolsey]] goes to the door and calls Gardiner<br>I find him a fit fellow. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Gardiner |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | (aside to Gardiner)<br>Give me your hand. Much joy and favour to you.<br>You are the [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s now. |
| GARDINER | (aside to Wolsey) But to be commanded<br>For ever by your grace, whose hand has raised me. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Come hither, Gardiner.<br>The [[archetypes/king\|King]] walks with Gardiner and whispers with him |
| CARDINAL CAMPEIUS | (to Wolsey)<br>My lord of York, was not one Doctor Pace<br>In this man’s place before him? |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | Yes, he was. |
| CARDINAL CAMPEIUS | Was he not held a learnèd man? |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | Yes, surely. |
| CARDINAL CAMPEIUS | Believe me, there’s an ill opinion spread then,<br>Even of yourself, lord Cardinal. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | How? Of me? |
| CARDINAL CAMPEIUS | They will not stick to say you envied him,<br>And fearing he would rise, he was so virtuous,<br>Kept him a foreign man still, which so grieved him<br>That he ran mad and died. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | Heav’n’s peace be with him—<br>That’s Christian care enough. For living murmurers<br>There’s places of rebuke. He was a [[archetypes/fool\|fool]],<br>For he would needs be virtuous.<br>(Gesturing towards Gardiner)<br>That good fellow,<br>If I command him, follows my appointment.<br>I will have none so near else. Learn this, brother:<br>We live not to be griped by meaner persons. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | (to Gardiner)<br>Deliver this with modesty to th’ [[characters/queen\|Queen]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Gardiner |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | The most convenient place that I can think of<br>For such receipt of learning is Blackfriars;<br>There ye shall meet about this weighty business.<br>My Wolsey, see it furnished. O, my lord,<br>Would it not grieve an able man to leave<br>So sweet a bedfellow? But [[concepts/conscience\|conscience]], [[concepts/conscience\|conscience]]—<br>O, ’tis a tender place, and I must leave her. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |

### Atto 2, Scena 3

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Anne Boleyn and an Old Lady |
| ANNE | Not for that neither. Here’s the pang that pinches—<br>His highness having lived so long with her, and she<br>So good a lady that no tongue could ever<br>Pronounce dishonour of her—by my life,<br>She never knew harm-doing—O now, after<br>So many courses of the sun enthroned,<br>Still growing in a majesty and pomp the which<br>To leave a thousandfold more bitter than<br>’Tis sweet at first t’acquire—after this process,<br>To give her the avaunt, it is a pity<br>Would move a [[archetypes/monster\|monster]]. |
| OLD LADY | Hearts of most hard temper<br>Melt and lament for her. |
| ANNE | O, God’s will! Much better<br>She ne’er had known pomp; though’t be temporal,<br>Yet if that quarrel, fortune, do divorce<br>It from the bearer, ’tis a sufferance panging<br>As [[motifs/soul\|soul]] and bodies severing. |
| OLD LADY | Alas, poor lady!<br>She’s a stranger now again. |
| ANNE | So much the more<br>Must pity drop upon her. Verily,<br>I swear, ’tis better to be lowly born<br>And range with humble livers in content<br>Than to be perked up in a glist’ring grief<br>And wear a golden sorrow. |
| OLD LADY | Our content<br>Is our best having. |
| ANNE | By my troth and maidenhead,<br>I would not be a [[characters/queen\|queen]]. |
| OLD LADY | Beshrew me, I would—<br>And venture maidenhead for’t; and so would you,<br>For all this spice of your hypocrisy.<br>You, that have so fair parts of woman on you,<br>Have, too, a woman’s heart which ever yet<br>Affected eminence, wealth, sovereignty;<br>Which, to say sooth, are blessings; and which gifts,<br>Saving your mincing, the capacity<br>Of your soft cheveril [[concepts/conscience\|conscience]] would receive<br>If you might please to stretch it. |
| ANNE | Nay, good troth. |
| OLD LADY | Yes, troth and troth. You would not be a [[characters/queen\|queen]]? |
| ANNE | No, not for all the riches under heaven. |
| OLD LADY | ’Tis strange. A threepence bowed would hire me,<br>Old as I am, to [[characters/queen\|queen]] it. But I pray you,<br>What think you of a duchess? Have you limbs<br>To bear that load of title? |
| ANNE | No, in [[concepts/truth\|truth]]. |
| OLD LADY | Then you are weakly made. Pluck off a little;<br>I would not be a young count in your way<br>For more than blushing comes to. If your back<br>Cannot vouchsafe this burden, ’tis too weak<br>Ever to get a boy. |
| ANNE | How you do talk!<br>I swear again, I would not be a [[characters/queen\|queen]]<br>For all the world. |
| OLD LADY | In [[concepts/faith\|faith]], for little England<br>You’d venture an emballing; I myself<br>Would for Caernarfonshire, although there ’longed<br>No more to th’ [[motifs/crown\|crown]] but that. Lo, who comes here? |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Lord Chamberlain |
| LORD CHAMBERLAIN | Good morrow, ladies. What were’t worth to know<br>The secret of your conference? |
| ANNE | My good lord,<br>Not your demand; it values not your asking.<br>Our mistress’ sorrows we were pitying. |
| LORD CHAMBERLAIN | It was a gentle business, and becoming<br>The action of good women. There is [[concepts/hope\|hope]]<br>All will be [[motifs/well\|well]]. |
| ANNE | Now I pray God, amen. |
| LORD CHAMBERLAIN | You bear a gentle mind, and heav’nly blessings<br>Follow such creatures. That you may, fair lady,<br>Perceive I speak sincerely, and high note’s<br>Ta’en of your many virtues, the [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s majesty<br>Commends his good opinion of you, and<br>Does purpose [[concepts/honour\|honour]] to you no less flowing<br>Than [[characters/marchioness\|Marchioness]] of Pembroke; to which title<br>A thousand pound a year annual support<br>Out of his grace he adds. |
| ANNE | I do not know<br>What kind of my obedience I should tender.<br>More than my all is nothing; nor my prayers<br>Are not words duly hallowed, nor my wishes<br>More worth than empty vanities; yet prayers and wishes<br>Are all I can return. Beseech your lordship,<br>Vouchsafe to speak my thanks and my obedience,<br>As from a blushing handmaid to his highness,<br>Whose health and royalty I pray for. |
| LORD CHAMBERLAIN | Lady,<br>I shall not fail t’approve the fair conceit<br>The [[archetypes/king\|King]] hath of you. (Aside) I have perused her [[motifs/well\|well]].<br>[[concepts/beauty\|Beauty]] and [[concepts/honour\|honour]] in her are so mingled<br>That they have caught the [[archetypes/king\|King]], and who knows yet<br>But from this lady may proceed a gem<br>To lighten all this isle. (To Anne) I’ll to the [[archetypes/king\|King]]<br>And say I spoke with you; |
| ANNE | My honoured lord. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit the Lord Chamberlain |
| OLD LADY | Why, this it is—see, see!<br>I have been begging sixteen years in [[settings/court\|court]],<br>Am yet a courtier beggarly, nor could<br>Come pat betwixt too early and too late<br>For any suit of pounds; and you—O, fate!—<br>A very fresh fish here—fie, fie upon<br>This compelled fortune!—have your mouth filled up<br>Before you open it. |
| ANNE | This is strange to me. |
| OLD LADY | How tastes it? Is it bitter? Forty pence, no.<br>There was a lady once—’tis an old story—<br>That would not be a [[characters/queen\|queen]], that would she not,<br>For all the mud in [[settings/egypt\|Egypt]]. Have you heard it? |
| ANNE | Come, you are pleasant. |
| OLD LADY | With your theme I could<br>O’ermount the lark. The [[characters/marchioness\|Marchioness]] of Pembroke?<br>A thousand pounds a year, for pure respect?<br>No other obligation? By my life,<br>That promises more thousands. [[concepts/honour\|Honour]]’s train<br>Is longer than his foreskirt. By this [[motifs/time\|time]]<br>I know your back will bear a duchess. Say,<br>Are you not stronger than you were? |
| ANNE | Good lady,<br>Make yourself mirth with your particular fancy,<br>And leave me out on’t. Would I had no being;<br>If this salute my [[motifs/blood\|blood]] a jot. It faints me<br>To think what follows.<br>The [[characters/queen\|Queen]] is comfortless, and we forgetful<br>In our long absence. Pray do not deliver<br>What here you’ve heard to her. |
| OLD LADY | What do you think me— |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |

### Atto 2, Scena 4

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Trumpets: sennet. Then cornetts. Enter two vergers with short silver wands; next them two Scribes in the habit of doctors; after them the Archbishop of Canterbury alone; after him the Bishops of [[characters/lincoln\|Lincoln]], Ely, Rochester, and Saint Asaph; next them, with some small distance, follows a gentleman bearing both the purse containing the great seal and a cardinal’s hat; then two priests bearing each a silver [[motifs/cross\|cross]]; then a gentleman usher, bare-headed, accompanied with a serjeant-at-arms bearing a silver mace; then two gentlemen bearing two great silver pillars; after them, side by side, the two cardinals, Wolsey and Campeius; then two noblemen with the sword and mace. The [[archetypes/king\|King]] ascends to his seat under the cloth of state; the two cardinals sit under him as judges; the [[characters/queen\|Queen]], attended by Griffith her gentleman usher, takes place some distance from the [[archetypes/king\|King]]; the Bishops place themselves on each side the [[settings/court\|court]] in the manner of a consistory; below them, the Scribes. The lords sit next the Bishops. The rest of the attendants stand in convenient order about the stage |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | Whilst our commission from Rome is read<br>Let [[motifs/silence\|silence]] be commanded. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | What’s the need?<br>It hath already publicly been read,<br>And on all sides th’authority allowed.<br>You may then spare that [[motifs/time\|time]]. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | Be’t so. Proceed. |
| SCRIBE | (to the Crier)<br>Say, ‘Henry, [[archetypes/king\|King]] of England, come into the [[settings/court\|court]]’. |
| CRIER | Henry, [[archetypes/king\|King]] of England, come into the [[settings/court\|court]]. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Here. |
| SCRIBE | (to the Crier)<br>Say, ‘Katherine, [[characters/queen\|Queen]] of England, come into the [[settings/court\|court]]’. |
| CRIER | Katherine, [[characters/queen\|Queen]] of England, come into the [[settings/court\|court]].<br>The [[characters/queen\|Queen]] makes no answer, but rises out of her chair, goes about the [[settings/court\|court]], comes to the [[archetypes/king\|King]], and kneels at his feet. Then she speaks |
| [[characters/queen-katherine\|QUEEN KATHERINE]] | Sir, I [[motifs/desire\|desire]] you do me right and justice,<br>And to bestow your pity on me; for<br>I am a most poor woman, and a stranger,<br>Born out of your dominions, having here<br>No judge indifferent, nor no more assurance<br>Of equal [[concepts/friendship\|friendship]] and proceeding. Alas, sir,<br>In what have I offended you? What cause<br>Hath my behaviour given to your displeasure<br>That thus you should proceed to put me off,<br>And take your good grace from me? Heaven witness<br>I have been to you a true and humble wife,<br>At all times to your will conformable,<br>Ever in [[concepts/fear\|fear]] to kindle your dislike,<br>Yea, subject to your countenance, glad or sorry<br>As I saw it inclined. When was the hour<br>I ever contradicted your [[motifs/desire\|desire]],<br>Or made it not mine too? Or which of your friends<br>Have I not strove to [[motifs/love\|love]], although I knew<br>He were mine enemy? What friend of mine<br>That had to him derived your anger did I<br>Continue in my liking? Nay, gave notice<br>He was from thence discharged? Sir, call to mind<br>That I have been your wife in this obedience<br>Upward of twenty years, and have been blessed<br>With many children by you. If, in the course<br>And process of this [[motifs/time\|time]], you can report—<br>And prove it, too—against mine [[concepts/honour\|honour]] aught,<br>My bond to wedlock, or my [[motifs/love\|love]] and duty<br>Against your sacred person, in God’s name<br>Turn me away, and let the foul‘st contempt<br>Shut door upon me, and so give me up<br>To the sharp’st kind of justice. Please you, sir,<br>The [[archetypes/king\|King]] your father was reputed for<br>A prince most prudent, of an excellent<br>And unmatched wit and judgement. [[characters/ferdinand\|Ferdinand]]<br>My father, [[archetypes/king\|King]] of Spain, was reckoned one<br>The wisest prince that there had reigned by many<br>A year before. It is not to be questioned<br>That they had gathered a wise council to them<br>Of every realm, that did debate this business,<br>Who deemed our [[motifs/marriage\|marriage]] lawful. Wherefore I humbly<br>Beseech you, sir, to spare me till I may<br>Be by my friends in Spain advised, whose counsel<br>I will implore. If not, i’th’ name of God,<br>Your pleasure be fulfilled. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | You have here, lady,<br>And of your choice, these reverend fathers, men<br>Of singular integrity and learning,<br>Yea, the elect o’th’ land, who are assembled<br>To plead your cause. It shall be therefore bootless<br>That longer you [[motifs/desire\|desire]] the [[settings/court\|court]], as [[motifs/well\|well]]<br>For your own quiet, as to rectify<br>What is unsettled in the [[archetypes/king\|King]]. |
| CARDINAL CAMPEIUS | His grace<br>Hath spoken [[motifs/well\|well]] and justly. Therefore, madam,<br>It’s fit this royal session do proceed,<br>And that without delay their arguments<br>Be now produced and heard. |
| [[characters/queen-katherine\|QUEEN KATHERINE]] | (to Wolsey) Lord Cardinal,<br>To you I speak. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | Your pleasure, madam. |
| [[characters/queen-katherine\|QUEEN KATHERINE]] | Sir,<br>I am about to weep, but thinking that<br>We are a [[characters/queen\|queen]], or long have dreamed so, certain<br>The daughter of a [[archetypes/king\|king]], my drops of [[motifs/tears\|tears]]<br>I’ll turn to sparks of [[motifs/fire\|fire]]. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | Be patient yet. |
| [[characters/queen-katherine\|QUEEN KATHERINE]] | I will when you are humble! Nay, before,<br>Or God will punish me. I do believe,<br>Induced by potent circumstances, that<br>You are mine enemy, and make my challenge<br>You shall not be my judge. For it is you<br>Have blown this coal betwixt my lord and me,<br>Which God’s dew quench.
