Therefore I say again,<br>I utterly abhor, yea, from my [[motifs/soul\|soul]],<br>Refuse you for my judge, whom yet once more<br>I hold my most malicious foe, and think not<br>At all a friend to [[concepts/truth\|truth]]. |
| CARDINAL WOISEY | I do profess<br>You speak not like yourself, who ever yet<br>Have stood to [[concepts/charity\|charity]], and displayed th‘effects<br>Of disposition gentle and of wisdom<br>O’er-topping woman’s power. Madam, you do me wrong.<br>I have no spleen against you, nor injustice<br>For you or any. How far I have proceeded,<br>Or how far further shall, is warranted<br>By a commission from the consistory,<br>Yea, the whole consistory of Rome. You charge me<br>That I ‘have blown this coal’. I do deny it.<br>The [[archetypes/king\|King]] is present. If it be known to him<br>That I gainsay my deed, how may he wound,<br>And worthily, my falsehood—yea, as much<br>As you have done my [[concepts/truth\|truth]]. If he know<br>That I am free of your report, he knows<br>I am not of your wrong. Therefore in him<br>It lies to cure me, and the cure is to<br>Remove these thoughts from you. The which before<br>His highness shall speak in, I do beseech<br>You, gracious madam, to unthink your speaking,<br>And to say so no more. |
| [[characters/queen-katherine\|QUEEN KATHERINE]] | My lord, my lord—<br>I am a simple woman, much too weak<br>T‘oppose your cunning. You’re meek and humblemouthed;<br>You sign your place and calling, in full seeming,<br>With meekness and [[concepts/humility\|humility]]—but your heart<br>Is crammed with arrogancy, spleen, and [[concepts/pride\|pride]].<br>You have by fortune and his highness’ favours<br>Gone slightly o’er low steps, and now are mounted<br>Where powers are your retainers, and your words,<br>Domestics to you, serve your will as’t please<br>Yourself pronounce their office. I must tell you,<br>You tender more your person’s [[concepts/honour\|honour]] than<br>Your high profession spiritual, that again<br>I do refuse you for my judge, and here,<br>Before you all, appeal unto the Pope,<br>To bring my whole cause ’fore his holiness,<br>And to be judged by him.<br>She curtsies to the [[archetypes/king\|King]] and begins to depart |
| CARDINAL CAMPEIUS | The [[characters/queen\|Queen]] is obstinate,<br>Stubborn to justice, apt to accuse it, and<br>Disdainful to be tried by’t. ’Tis not [[motifs/well\|well]].<br>She’s going away. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | (to the Crier) Call her again. |
| CRIER | Katherine, [[characters/queen\|Queen]] of England, come into the [[settings/court\|court]]. |
| GRIFFITH | (to the [[characters/queen\|Queen]]) Madam, you are called back. |
| [[characters/queen-katherine\|QUEEN KATHERINE]] | What need you note it? Pray you keep your way.<br>When you are called, return. Now the Lord help.<br>They vex me past my patience. Pray you, pass on.<br>I will not tarry; no, nor ever more<br>Upon this business my appearance make<br>In any of their courts. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt [[characters/queen-katherine\|Queen Katherine]] and her attendants |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Go thy ways, Kate.<br>That man i’th’ world who shall report he has<br>A better wife, let him in naught be trusted<br>For speaking false in that. Thou art alone—<br>If thy rare qualities, sweet gentleness,<br>Thy meekness saint-like, wife-like government,<br>Obeying in commanding, and thy parts<br>Sovereign and pious else could speak thee out—<br>The [[characters/queen\|queen]] of earthly queens. She’s noble born,<br>And like her true nobility she has<br>Carried herself towards me. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | Most gracious sir,<br>In humblest manner I require your highness<br>That it shall please you to declare in hearing<br>Of all these ears—for where I am robbed and bound,<br>There must I be unloosed, although not there<br>At once and fully satisfied—whether ever I<br>Did broach this business to your highness, or<br>Laid any scruple in your way which might<br>Induce you to the question on’t, or ever<br>Have to you, but with thanks to God for such<br>A royal lady, spake one the least word that might<br>Be to the prejudice of her present state,<br>Or touch of her good person? |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | My lord Cardinal,<br>I do excuse you; yea, upon mine [[concepts/honour\|honour]],<br>I free you from’t. You are not to be taught<br>That you have many enemies that know not<br>Why they are so, but, like to village curs,<br>Bark when their fellows do. By some of these<br>The [[characters/queen\|Queen]] is put in anger. You’re excused.<br>But will you be more justified? You ever<br>Have wished the sleeping of this business, never desired<br>It to be stirred, but oft have hindered, oft,<br>The passages made toward it. On my [[concepts/honour\|honour]]<br>I speak my good lord Card’nal to this point,<br>And thus far clear him. Now, what moved me to’t,<br>I will be bold with [[motifs/time\|time]] and your attention.<br>Then mark th‘inducement. Thus it came—give heed to’t.<br>My [[concepts/conscience\|conscience]] first received a tenderness,<br>Scruple, and prick, on certain speeches uttered<br>By th’ Bishop of Bayonne, then French Ambassador,<br>Who had been hither sent on the debating<br>A [[motifs/marriage\|marriage]] ’twixt the Duke of Orléans and<br>Our daughter Mary. I‘th’ progress of this business,<br>Ere a determinate resolution, he—<br>I mean the Bishop—did require a respite<br>Wherein he might the [[archetypes/king\|King]] his lord advertise<br>Whether our daughter were legitimate,<br>Respecting this our [[motifs/marriage\|marriage]] with the dowager,<br>Sometimes our brother’s wife. This respite shook<br>The bosom of my [[concepts/conscience\|conscience]], entered me,<br>Yea, with a spitting power, and made to tremble<br>The region of my breast; which forced such way<br>That many mazed considerings did throng<br>And prest in with this caution. First, methought<br>I stood not in the smile of heaven, who had<br>Commanded [[concepts/nature\|nature]] that my lady’s womb,<br>If it conceived a male [[archetypes/child\|child]] by me, should<br>Do no more offices of life to’t than<br>The [[motifs/grave\|grave]] does yield to th’ dead. For her male issue<br>Or died where they were made, or shortly after<br>This world had aired them. Hence I took a thought<br>This was a judgement on me that my kingdom,<br>[[motifs/well\|Well]] worthy the best heir o’th’ world, should not<br>Be gladded in’t by me. Then follows that<br>I weighed the danger which my realms stood in<br>By this my issue’s fail, and that gave to me<br>Many a groaning throe. Thus hulling in<br>The wild sea of my [[concepts/conscience\|conscience]], I did steer<br>Toward this remedy, whereupon we are<br>Now present here together—that’s to say<br>I meant to rectify my [[concepts/conscience\|conscience]], which<br>I then did feel full sick, and yet not [[motifs/well\|well]],<br>By all the reverend fathers of the land<br>And doctors learned. First I began in private<br>With you, my lord of [[characters/lincoln\|Lincoln]]. You remember<br>How under my oppression I did reek<br>When I first moved you. |
| [[characters/lincoln\|LINCOLN]] | Very [[motifs/well\|well]], my liege. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | I have spoke long. Be pleased yourself to say<br>How far you satisfied me. |
| [[characters/lincoln\|LINCOLN]] | So please your highness,<br>The question did at first so stagger me,<br>Bearing a state of mighty moment in’t<br>And consequence of dread, that I committed<br>The daring’st counsel which I had to [[concepts/doubt\|doubt]],<br>And did entreat your highness to this course<br>Which you are running here. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | (to Canterbury)<br>I then moved you,<br>My lord of Canterbury, and got your leave<br>To make this present summons. Unsolicited<br>I left no reverend person in this [[settings/court\|court]],<br>But by particular consent proceeded<br>Under your hands and seals. Therefore, go on,<br>For no dislike i‘th’ world against the person<br>Of the good [[characters/queen\|Queen]], but the sharp thorny points<br>Of my alleged reasons, drives this forward.<br>Prove but our [[motifs/marriage\|marriage]] lawful, by my life<br>And kingly dignity, we are contented<br>To wear our mortal state to come with her,<br>Katherine, our [[characters/queen\|queen]], before the primest creature<br>That’s paragoned o’th’ world. |
| CARDINAL CAMPEIUS | So please your highness,<br>The [[characters/queen\|Queen]] being absent, ’tis a needful fitness<br>That we adjourn this [[settings/court\|court]] till further day.<br>Meanwhile must be an earnest motion<br>Made to the [[characters/queen\|Queen]] to call back her appeal<br>She intends unto his holiness. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | (aside)<br>I may perceive<br>These cardinals trifle with me. I abhor<br>This dilatory sloth and tricks of Rome.<br>My learned and [[motifs/well\|well]]-belovèd servant, [[characters/cranmer\|Cranmer]],<br>Prithee return. With thy approach I know<br>My comfort comes along. (Aloud) Break up the [[settings/court\|court]].<br>I say, set on. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt in manner as they entered |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | 3.1 Enter [[characters/queen-katherine\|Queen Katherine]] and her women, as at work |
| [[characters/queen-katherine\|QUEEN KATHERINE]] | Take thy lute, wench. My [[motifs/soul\|soul]] grows sad with troubles.<br>Sing, and disperse ’em if thou canst. Leave working. |
| GENTLEWOMAN | (sings)<br>Orpheus with his lute made trees,<br>And the mountain tops that freeze,<br>Bow themselves when he did sing.<br>To his music plants and [[motifs/flowers\|flowers]]<br>Ever sprung, as sun and showers<br>There had made a lasting [[motifs/spring\|spring]].<br>Everything that heard him play,<br>Even the billows of the sea,<br>Hung their heads, and then lay by.<br>In sweet music is such art,<br>Killing care and grief of heart<br>Fall asleep, or hearing, die. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Griffith, a gentleman |
| [[characters/queen-katherine\|QUEEN KATHERINE]] | How now? |
| *(didascalia)* | GRIFFITH |
| [[characters/queen-katherine\|QUEEN KATHERINE]] | An’t please your grace, the two great cardinals<br>Wait in the presence. |
| [[characters/queen-katherine\|QUEEN KATHERINE]] | Would they speak with me? |
| *(didascalia)* | GRIFFITH |
| [[characters/queen-katherine\|QUEEN KATHERINE]] | They willed me say so, madam. |
| [[characters/queen-katherine\|QUEEN KATHERINE]] | Pray their graces<br>To come near. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Griffith |
| [[characters/queen-katherine\|QUEEN KATHERINE]] | What can be their business<br>With me, a poor weak woman, fall’n from favour?<br>I do not like their coming, now I think on’t;<br>They should be good men, their affairs as righteous—<br>But all hoods make not [[characters/monks\|monks]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the two cardinals, Wolsey and Campeius, ushered by Griffith |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | Peace to your highness. |
| [[characters/queen-katherine\|QUEEN KATHERINE]] | Your graces find me here part of a housewife—<br>I would be all, against the worst may happen.<br>What are your pleasures with me, reverend lords? |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | May it please you, noble madam, to withdraw<br>Into your private chamber, we shall give you<br>The full cause of our coming. |
| [[characters/queen-katherine\|QUEEN KATHERINE]] | Speak it here.<br>There’s nothing I have done yet, o’ my [[concepts/conscience\|conscience]],<br>Deserves a corner. Would all other women<br>Could speak this with as free a [[motifs/soul\|soul]] as I do.<br>My lords, I care not—so much I am happy<br>Above a number—if my actions<br>Were tried by ev’ry tongue, ev’ry eye saw ’em,<br>Envy and base opinion set against ’em,<br>I know my life so even. If your business<br>Seek me out and that way I am wife in,<br>Out with it boldly. [[concepts/truth\|Truth]] loves open dealing. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | Tanta est erga te mentis integritas, Regina serenissima— |
| [[characters/queen-katherine\|QUEEN KATHERINE]] | O, good my lord, no Latin.<br>I am not such a truant since my coming<br>As not to know the language I have lived in.<br>A strange tongue makes my cause more strange<br>suspicious—<br>Pray, speak in English. Here are some will thank you,<br>If you speak [[concepts/truth\|truth]], for their poor mistress’ sake.<br>Believe me, she has had much wrong. Lord Cardinal,<br>The willing’st sin I ever yet committed<br>May be absolved in English. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | Noble lady,<br>I am sorry my integrity should breed—so<br>And service to his majesty and you—<br>So deep suspicion, where all [[concepts/faith\|faith]] was meant.<br>We come not by the way of accusation,<br>To taint that [[concepts/honour\|honour]] every good tongue blesses,<br>Nor to betray you any way to sorrow—<br>You have too much, good lady—but to know<br>How you stand minded in the weighty difference<br>Between the [[archetypes/king\|King]] and you, and to deliver,<br>Like free and honest men, our just opinions<br>And comforts to your cause. |
| CARDINAL CAMPEIUS | Most honoured madam,<br>My lord of York, out of his noble [[concepts/nature\|nature]],<br>Zeal, and obedience he still bore your grace,<br>Forgetting, like a good man, your late censure<br>Both of his [[concepts/truth\|truth]] and him—which was too far—<br>Offers, as I do, in a sign of peace,<br>His service and his counsel. |
| [[characters/queen-katherine\|QUEEN KATHERINE]] | (aside)<br>To betray me.<br>(Aloud) My lords, I thank you both for your good<br>wills.<br>Ye speak like honest men—pray God ye prove so.<br>But how to make ye suddenly an answer<br>In such a point of weight, so near mine [[concepts/honour\|honour]]—<br>More near my life, I [[concepts/fear\|fear]]—with my weak wit,<br>And to such men of gravity and learning,<br>In [[concepts/truth\|truth]] I know not. I was set at work<br>Among my maids, full little—God knows—looking<br>Either for such men or such business.<br>For her sake that I have been—for I feel<br>The last fit of my [[concepts/greatness\|greatness]]—good your graces,<br>Let me have [[motifs/time\|time]] and counsel for my cause.<br>Alas, I am a woman friendless, hopeless. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | Madam, you wrong the [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s [[motifs/love\|love]] with these fears.<br>Your hopes and friends are infinite. |
| [[characters/queen-katherine\|QUEEN KATHERINE]] | In England<br>But little for my profit. Can you think, lords,<br>That any Englishman dare give me counsel,<br>Or be a known friend ’gainst his highness’ pleasure—<br>Though he be grown so desperate to be honest—<br>And live a subject? Nay, forsooth, my friends,<br>They that must weigh out my afflictions,<br>They that my trust must grow to, live not here.<br>They are, as all my other comforts, far hence,<br>In mine own country, lords. |
| CARDINAL CAMPEIUS | I would your grace<br>Would leave your griefs and take my counsel. |
| [[characters/queen-katherine\|QUEEN KATHERINE]] | How, sir? |
| CARDINAL CAMPEIUS | Put your main cause into the [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s protection.<br>He’s loving and most gracious. ’Twill be much<br>Both for your [[concepts/honour\|honour]] better and your cause,<br>For if the trial of the law o’ertake ye<br>You’ll part away disgraced. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | (to the [[characters/queen\|Queen]]) He tells you rightly. |
| [[characters/queen-katherine\|QUEEN KATHERINE]] | Ye tell me what ye wish for both—my ruin.<br>Is this your Christian counsel? Out upon ye!<br>Heaven is above all yet—there sits a judge<br>That no [[archetypes/king\|king]] can corrupt. |
| CARDINAL CAMPEIUS | Your rage mistakes us. |
| [[characters/queen-katherine\|QUEEN KATHERINE]] | The more shame for ye! Holy men I thought ye,<br>Upon my [[motifs/soul\|soul]], two reverend cardinal virtues—<br>But cardinal sins and hollow hearts I [[concepts/fear\|fear]] ye.<br>Mend ’em, for shame, my lords! Is this your comfort?<br>The cordial that ye bring a wretched lady,<br>A woman lost among ye, laughed at, scorned?<br>I will not wish ye half my miseries—<br>I have more [[concepts/charity\|charity]]. But say I warned ye.<br>Take heed, for, heaven’s sake take heed, lest at once<br>The burden of my sorrows fall upon ye. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | Madam, this is a mere distraction.<br>You turn the good we offer into envy. |
| [[characters/queen-katherine\|QUEEN KATHERINE]] | Ye turn me into nothing. Woe upon ye,<br>And all such false professors. Would you have me—<br>If you have any justice, any pity,<br>If ye be anything but churchmen’s habits—<br>Put my sick cause into his hands that hates me?<br>Alas, he’s banished me his bed already—<br>His [[motifs/love\|love]], too, long ago. I am old, my lords,<br>And all the fellowship I hold now with him<br>Is only my obedience. What can happen<br>To me above this wretchedness? All your studies<br>Make me accursed like this. |
| CARDINAL CAMPEIUS | Your fears are worse. |
| [[characters/queen-katherine\|QUEEN KATHERINE]] | Have I lived thus long—let me speak myself,<br>Since virtue finds no friends—a wife, a true one?<br>A woman, I dare say, without vainglory,<br>Never yet branded with suspicion?<br>Have I with all my full affections<br>Still met the [[archetypes/king\|King]], loved him next heav‘n, obeyed him,<br>Been out of fondness superstitious to him,<br>Almost forgot my prayers to content him?<br>And am I thus rewarded? ’Tis not [[motifs/well\|well]], lords.<br>Bring me a constant woman to her husband,<br>One that ne’er dreamed a joy beyond his pleasure,<br>And to that woman when she has done most,<br>Yet will I add an [[concepts/honour\|honour]], a great patience. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | Madam, you wander from the good we aim at. |
| [[characters/queen-katherine\|QUEEN KATHERINE]] | My lord, I dare not make myself so guilty<br>To give up willingly that noble title<br>Your master wed me to. Nothing but [[motifs/death\|death]]<br>Shall e’er divorce my dignities. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | Pray, hear me. |
| [[characters/queen-katherine\|QUEEN KATHERINE]] | Would I had never trod this English earth,<br>Or felt the flatteries that grow upon it.<br>Ye have angels’ faces, but heaven knows your hearts.<br>What will become of me now, wretched lady?<br>I am the most unhappy woman living.<br>(To her women) Alas, poor wenches, where are now<br>your fortunes?<br>Shipwrecked upon a kingdom where no pity,<br>No friends, no [[concepts/hope\|hope]], no kindred weep for me?<br>Almost no [[motifs/grave\|grave]] allowed me? Like the lily,<br>That once was mistress of the field and flourished,<br>I’ll hang my head and perish. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | If your grace<br>Could but be brought to know our ends are honest,<br>You’d feel more comfort. Why should we, good lady,<br>Upon what cause, wrong you? Alas, our places,<br>The way of our profession, is against it.<br>We are to cure such sorrows, not to sow ’em.<br>For goodness’ sake, consider what you do,<br>How you may hurt yourself, ay, utterly<br>Grow from the [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s acquaintance by this carriage.<br>The hearts of princes kiss obedience,<br>So much they [[motifs/love\|love]] it, but to stubborn spirits<br>They swell and grow as terrible as storms.<br>I know you have a gentle noble temper,<br>A [[motifs/soul\|soul]] as even as a calm. Pray, think us<br>Those we profess—peacemakers, friends, and servants. |
| CARDINAL CAMPEIUS | Madam, you’ll find it so. You wrong your virtues<br>With these weak women’s fears. A noble spirit,<br>As yours was put into you, ever casts<br>Such doubts as false coin from it. The [[archetypes/king\|King]] loves you.<br>Beware you lose it not. For us, if you please<br>To trust us in your business, we are ready<br>To use our utmost studies in your service. |
| QUEENS KATHERINE | Do what ye will, my lords, and pray forgive me.<br>If I have used myself unmannerly,<br>You know I am a woman, lacking wit<br>To make a seemly answer to such persons.<br>Pray do my service to his majesty.<br>He has my heart yet, and shall have my prayers<br>While I shall have my life. Come, reverend fathers,<br>Bestow your counsels on me. She now begs<br>That little thought, when she set footing here,<br>She should have bought her dignities so dear. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |

### Atto 3, Scena 2

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Duke of Norfolk, the [[characters/duke-of-suffolk\|Duke of Suffolk]], Lord Surrey, and the Lord Chamberlain |
| NORFOLK | If you will now unite in your complaints,<br>And force them with a constancy, the Cardinal<br>Cannot stand under them. If you omit<br>The offer of this [[motifs/time\|time]], I cannot promise<br>But that you shall sustain more new disgraces<br>With these you bear already. |
| SURREY | I am joyful<br>To meet the least occasion that may give me<br>Remembrance of my father-in-law the Duke,<br>To be revenged on him. |
| SUFFOLK | Which of the peers<br>Have uncontemned gone by him, or at least<br>Strangely neglected? When did he regard<br>The stamp of nobleness in any person<br>Out of himself? |
| LORD CHAMBERLAIN | My lords, you speak your pleasures.<br>What he deserves of you and me I know;<br>What we can do to him—though now the [[motifs/time\|time]]<br>Gives way to us—I much [[concepts/fear\|fear]]. If you cannot<br>Bar his access to th’ [[archetypes/king\|King]], never attempt<br>Anything on him, for he hath a witchcraft<br>Over the [[archetypes/king\|King]] in’s tongue. |
| NORFOLK | O, [[concepts/fear\|fear]] him not.<br>His spell in that is out. The [[archetypes/king\|King]] hath found<br>Matter against him that for ever mars<br>The honey of his language. No, he’s settled,<br>Not to come off, in his displeasure. |
| SURREY | Sir,<br>I should be glad to hear such news as this<br>Once every hour. |
| NORFOLK | Believe it, this is true.<br>In the divorce his contrary proceedings<br>Are all unfolded, wherein he appears<br>As I would wish mine enemy. |
| SURREY | How came<br>His practices to light? |
| SUFFOLK | Most strangely. |
| SURREY | O, how, how? |
| SUFFOLK | The Cardinal’s letters to the Pope miscarried,<br>And came to th’eye o’th’ [[archetypes/king\|King]], wherein was read<br>How that the Cardinal did entreat his holiness<br>To stay the judgement o‘th’ divorce, for if<br>It did take place, ‘I do’, quoth he, ‘perceive<br>My [[archetypes/king\|king]] is tangled in affection to<br>A creature of the [[characters/queen\|Queen]]’s, [[characters/lady-anne\|Lady Anne]] Boleyn’. |
| SURREY | Has the [[archetypes/king\|King]] this? |
| SUFFOLK | Believe it. |
| SURREY | Will this work? |
| LORD CHAMBERLAIN | The [[archetypes/king\|King]] in this perceives him how he coasts<br>And hedges his own way. But in this point<br>All his tricks founder, and he brings his physic<br>After his patient’s [[motifs/death\|death]]. The [[archetypes/king\|King]] already<br>Hath married the fair lady. |
| SURREY | Would he had. |
| SUFFOLK | May you be happy in your wish, my lord,<br>For I profess you have it. |
| SURREY | Now all my joy<br>Trace the conjunction. |
| SUFFOLK | My amen to’t. |
| NORFOLK | All men’s. |
| SUFFOLK | There’s order given for her coronation.<br>Marry, this is yet but young, and may be left<br>To some ears unrecounted. But, my lords,<br>She is a gallant creature, and complete<br>In mind and feature. I persuade me, from her<br>Will fall some blessing to this land which shall<br>In it be memorized. |
| SURREY | But will the [[archetypes/king\|King]]<br>Digest this letter of the Cardinal’s?<br>The Lord forbid! |
| NORFOLK | Marry, amen. |
| SUFFOLK | No, no—<br>There be more wasps that buzz about his nose<br>Will make this sting the sooner. Cardinal Campeius<br>Is stol’n away to Rome; hath ta’en no leave;<br>Has left the cause o’th’ [[archetypes/king\|King]] unhandled, and<br>Is posted as the agent of our Cardinal<br>To second all his plot. I do assure you<br>The [[archetypes/king\|King]] cried ‘Ha!’ at this. |
| LORD CHAMBERLAIN | Now God incense him,<br>And let him cry ‘Ha!’ louder. |
| NORFOLK | But, my lord,<br>When returns [[characters/cranmer\|Cranmer]]? |
| SUFFOLK | He is returned in his opinions, which<br>Have satisfied the [[archetypes/king\|King]] for his divorce,<br>Together with all famous colleges,<br>Almost, in Christendom. Shortly, I believe,<br>His second [[motifs/marriage\|marriage]] shall be published, and<br>Her coronation. Katherine no more<br>Shall be called ‘[[characters/queen\|Queen]]’, but ‘Princess Dowager’,<br>And ‘widow to Prince [[characters/arthur\|Arthur]]’. |
| NORFOLK | This same [[characters/cranmer\|Cranmer]]’s<br>A worthy fellow, and hath ta’en much [[motifs/pain\|pain]]<br>In the [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s business. |
| SUFFOLK | He has, and we shall see him<br>For it an archbishop. |
| NORFOLK | So I hear. |
| SUFFOLK | ’Tis so. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|Cardinal Wolsey]] and Cromwell |
| SUFFOLK | The Cardinal. |
| NORFOLK | Observe, observe—he’s moody.<br>They stand apart and observe Wolsey and<br>Cromwell |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | (to Cromwell)<br>The packet, Cromwell—gave’t you the [[archetypes/king\|King]]?<br>CROMWELI,<br>To his own hand, in’s bedchamber. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | Looked he<br>O’th’ inside of the paper?<br>CROMWELL,<br>Presently<br>He did unseal them, and the first he viewed<br>He did it with a serious mind; a heed<br>Was in his countenance. You he bade<br>Attend him here this morning. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | Is he ready<br>To come abroad? |
| CROMWELL | I think by this he is. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | Leave me a while. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Cromwell |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | (Aside) It shall be to the Duchess of Alençon,<br>The French [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s sister—he shall marry her.<br>Anne Boleyn? No, I’ll no Anne Boleyns for him.<br>There’s more in’t than fair visage. Boleyn?<br>No, we’ll no Boleyns. Speedily I wish<br>To hear from Rome. The [[characters/marchioness\|Marchioness]] of Pembroke?<br>The nobles speak among themselves |
| NORFOLK | He’s discontented. |
| SUFFOLK | Maybe he hears the [[archetypes/king\|King]]<br>Does whet his anger to him. |
| SURREY | Sharp enough,<br>Lord, for thy justice. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | (aside)<br>The late [[characters/queen\|Queen]]’s gentlewoman? A knight’s daughter<br>To be her mistress’ mistress? The [[characters/queen\|Queen]]’s [[characters/queen\|queen]]?<br>This candle burns not clear; ‘tis I must snuff it,<br>Then out it goes. What though I know her virtuous<br>And [[motifs/well\|well]] deserving? Yet I know her for<br>A spleeny Lutheran, and not wholesome to<br>Our cause, that she should lie i’th’ bosom of<br>Our hard-ruled [[archetypes/king\|King]]. Again, there is sprung up<br>An heretic, an arch-one, [[characters/cranmer\|Cranmer]], one<br>Hath crawled into the favour of the [[archetypes/king\|King]]<br>And is his oracle.<br>The nobles speak among themselves |
| NORFOLK | He is vexed at something. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[archetypes/king\|King]] Henry reading a schedule, and Lovell with him |
| SURREY | I would ’twere something that would fret the string,<br>The master-cord on’s heart! |
| SUFFOLK | The [[archetypes/king\|King]], the [[archetypes/king\|King]]! |
| *(didascalia)* | [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY aside |
| SUFFOLK | What piles of wealth hath he accumulated<br>To his own portion? And what expense by th’ hour<br>Seems to flow from him? How i’th’ name of thrift<br>Does he rake this together? (To the nobles) Now, my lords,<br>Saw you the Cardinal? |
| NORFOLK | My lord, we have<br>Stood here observing him. Some strange commotion<br>Is in his brain. He bites his lip, and starts,<br>Stops on a sudden, looks upon the ground,<br>Then lays his finger on his temple, straight<br>Springs out into fast gait, then stops again,<br>Strikes his breast hard, and anon he casts<br>His eye against the [[motifs/moon\|moon]]. In most strange postures<br>We have seen him set himself. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | It may [[motifs/well\|well]] be<br>There is a mutiny in’s mind. This morning<br>Papers of state he sent me to peruse<br>As I required, and wot you what I found<br>There, on my [[concepts/conscience\|conscience]] put unwittingly?<br>Forsooth, an inventory thus importing<br>The several parcels of his plate, his treasure,<br>Rich stuffs, and ornaments of household which<br>I find at such proud rate that it outspeaks<br>Possession of a subject. |
| NORFOLK | It’s heaven’s will.<br>Some spirit put this paper in the packet<br>To bless your eye withal. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | If we did think<br>His contemplation were above the earth<br>And fixed on spiritual object, he should still<br>Dwell in his musings. But I am afraid<br>His thinkings are below the [[motifs/moon\|moon]], not worth<br>His serious considering.<br>The [[archetypes/king\|King]] takes his seat and whispers with Lovell, who then goes to the Cardinal |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | Heaven forgive me! |
| *(didascalia)* | To the [[archetypes/king\|King]] Ever God bless your highness! |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Good my lord,<br>You are full of heavenly stuff, and bear the inventory<br>Of your best graces in your mind, the which<br>You were now running o’er. You have scarce [[motifs/time\|time]]<br>To steal from spiritual leisure a brief span<br>To keep your earthly audit. Sure, in that,<br>I deem you an ill husband, and am glad<br>To have you therein my companion. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | Sir,<br>For holy offices I have a [[motifs/time\|time]]; a [[motifs/time\|time]]<br>To think upon the part of business which<br>I bear i’th’ state; and [[concepts/nature\|nature]] does require<br>Her times of preservation which, perforce,<br>I, her frail son, amongst my brethren mortal,<br>Must give my tendance to. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | You have said [[motifs/well\|well]]. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | And ever may your highness yoke together,<br>As I will lend you cause, my doing [[motifs/well\|well]]<br>With my [[motifs/well\|well]]-saying. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | ’Tis [[motifs/well\|well]] said again,<br>And ’tis a kind of good deed to say [[motifs/well\|well]]—<br>And yet words are no deeds. My father loved you.<br>He said he did, and with his deed did [[motifs/crown\|crown]]<br>His word upon you. Since I had my office,<br>I have kept you next my heart, have not alone<br>Employed you where high profits might come [[motifs/home\|home]],<br>But pared my present havings to bestow<br>My bounties upon you. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | (aside) What should this mean? |
| *(didascalia)* | SURREY aside |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | The Lord increase this business! |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Have I not made you<br>The prime man of the state? I pray you tell me<br>If what I now pronounce you have found true,<br>And, if you may confess it, say withal<br>If you are bound to us or no. What say you? |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | My sovereign, I confess your royal graces<br>Showered on me daily have been more than could<br>My studied purposes requite, which went<br>Beyond all man’s endeavours. My endeavours<br>Have ever come too short of my desires,<br>Yet filed with my abilities. Mine own ends<br>Have been mine so that evermore they pointed<br>To th’ good of your most sacred person and<br>The profit of the state. For your great graces<br>Heaped upon me, poor undeserver, I<br>Can nothing render but allegiant thanks,<br>My prayers to heaven for you, my loyalty,<br>Which ever has and ever shall be growing,<br>Till [[motifs/death\|death]], that [[motifs/winter\|winter]], kill it. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Fairly answered.<br>A loyal and obedient subject is<br>Therein illustrated. The [[concepts/honour\|honour]] of it<br>Does pay the act of it, as, i‘th’ contrary,<br>The foulness is the punishment. I presume<br>That as my hand has opened bounty to you,<br>My heart dropped [[motifs/love\|love]], my power rained [[concepts/honour\|honour]], more<br>On you than any, so your hand and heart,<br>Your brain, and every function of your power,<br>Should, notwithstanding that your bond of duty,<br>As ’twere in [[motifs/love\|love]]’s particular, be more<br>To me, your friend, than any. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | I do profess<br>That for your highness’ good I ever laboured<br>More than mine own; that am, have, and will be—<br>Though all the world should crack their duty to you,<br>And throw it from their [[motifs/soul\|soul]], though perils did<br>Abound, as thick as thought could make ’em, and<br>Appear in forms more horrid—yet, my duty,<br>As doth a rock against the chiding flood,<br>Should the approach of this wild [[motifs/river\|river]] break,<br>And stand unshaken yours. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | ’Tis nobly spoken.<br>Take notice, lords, he has a loyal breast,<br>For you have seen him open’t. (To Wolsey) Read o’er this,<br>He gives him a paper<br>And after this (giving him another paper), and then to<br>breakfast with<br>What appetite you have. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit [[archetypes/king\|King]] Henry, frowning upon the |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Cardinal. The nobles throng after<br>the [[archetypes/king\|King]], smiling and whispering |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | What should this mean?<br>What sudden anger’s this? How have I reaped it?<br>He parted frowning from me, as if ruin<br>Leaped from his eyes. So looks the chafed [[motifs/lion\|lion]]<br>Upon the daring huntsman that has galled him,<br>Then makes him nothing. I must read this paper—<br>I [[concepts/fear\|fear]], the story of his anger.<br>He reads one of the papers<br>’Tis so.<br>This paper has undone me. ’Tis th‘account<br>Of all that world of wealth I have drawn together<br>For mine own ends—indeed, to gain the popedom,<br>And fee my friends in Rome. O negligence,<br>Fit for a [[archetypes/fool\|fool]] to fall by! What [[motifs/cross\|cross]] devil<br>Made me put this main secret in the packet<br>I sent the [[archetypes/king\|King]]? Is there no way to cure this?<br>No new device to beat this from his brains?<br>I know ’twill stir him strongly. Yet I know<br>A way, if it take right, in spite of fortune<br>Will bring me off again. What’s this?<br>He reads the other paper<br>‘To th’ Pope’?<br>The letter, as I live, with all the business<br>I writ to’s holiness. Nay then, farewell.<br>I have touched the highest point of all my [[concepts/greatness\|greatness]],<br>And from that full meridian of my glory<br>I haste now to my setting. I shall fall<br>Like a bright exhalation in the evening,<br>And no man see me more. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter to [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|Cardinal Wolsey]] the Dukes of Norfolk and |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | Suffolk, the [[characters/earl-of-surrey\|Earl of Surrey]], and the Lord Chamberlain |
| NORFOLK | Hear the [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s pleasure, Cardinal, who commands you<br>To render up the great seal presently<br>Into our hands, and to confine yourself<br>To Ashen House, my lord of Winchester’s,<br>Till you hear further from his highness. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | Stay—<br>Where’s your commission, lords? Words cannot carry<br>Authority so weighty. |
| SUFFOLK | Who dare [[motifs/cross\|cross]] ’em<br>Bearing the [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s will from his mouth expressly? |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | Till I find more than will or words to do it—<br>I mean your malice—know, officious lords,<br>I dare and must deny it. Now I feel<br>Of what coarse metal ye are moulded—envy.<br>How eagerly ye follow my disgraces<br>As if it fed ye, and how sleek and wanton<br>Ye appear in everything may bring my ruin!<br>Follow your envious courses, men of malice.<br>You have Christian warrant for ’em, and no [[concepts/doubt\|doubt]]<br>In [[motifs/time\|time]] will find their fit rewards. That seal<br>You ask with such a violence, the [[archetypes/king\|King]],<br>Mine and your master, with his own hand gave me,<br>Bade me enjoy it, with the place and honours,<br>During my life; and, to confirm his goodness,<br>Tied it by letters patents. Now, who’ll take it? |
| SURREY | The [[archetypes/king\|King]] that gave it. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | It must be himself then. |
| SURREY | Thou art a proud traitor, priest. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | Proud lord, thou liest. |
| *(didascalia)* | Within these forty hours Surrey durst better |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | Have burnt that tongue than said so. |
| SURREY | Thy [[concepts/ambition\|ambition]],<br>Thou scarlet sin, robbed this bewailing land<br>Of noble [[characters/buckingham\|Buckingham]], my father-in-law.<br>The heads of all thy brother cardinals<br>With thee and all thy best parts bound together<br>Weighed not a hair of his. Plague of your policy,<br>You sent me deputy for Ireland,<br>Far from his succour, from the [[archetypes/king\|King]], from all<br>That might have [[concepts/mercy\|mercy]] on the fault thou gav’st him;<br>Whilst your great goodness, out of holy pity,<br>Absolved him with an axe. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | This, and all else<br>This talking lord can lay upon my credit,<br>I answer is most false. The Duke by law<br>Found his deserts. How [[archetypes/innocent\|innocent]] I was<br>From any private malice in his end,<br>His noble jury and foul cause can witness.<br>If I loved many words, lord, I should tell you<br>You have as little honesty as [[concepts/honour\|honour]],<br>That in the way of loyalty and [[concepts/truth\|truth]]<br>Toward the [[archetypes/king\|King]], my ever royal master,<br>Dare mate a sounder man than Surrey can be,<br>And all that [[motifs/love\|love]] his follies. |
| SURREY | By my [[motifs/soul\|soul]],<br>Your long coat, priest, protects you; thou shouldst feel<br>My sword i’th’ life-[[motifs/blood\|blood]] of thee else. My lords,<br>Can ye endure to hear this arrogance,<br>And from this fellow? If we live thus tamely,<br>To be thus jaded by a piece of scarlet,<br>Farewell nobility. Let his grace go forward<br>And dare us with his cap, like larks. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | All goodness<br>Is [[motifs/poison\|poison]] to thy stomach. |
| SURREY | Yes, that goodness<br>Of gleaning all the land’s wealth into one,<br>Into your own hands, Card’nal, by extortion;<br>The goodness of your intercepted packets<br>You writ to th’ Pope against the [[archetypes/king\|King]]; your<br>goodness—<br>Since you provoke me—shall be most notorious.<br>My lord of Norfolk, as you are truly noble,<br>As you respect the common good, the state<br>Of our despised nobility, our issues—<br>Whom if he live will scarce be gentlemen—<br>Produce the grand sum of his sins, the articles<br>Collected from his life. (To Wolsey) I’ll startle you<br>Worse than the sacring-bell when the brown wench<br>Lay kissing in your arms, lord Cardinal. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] aside |
| SURREY | How much, methinks, I could despise this man,<br>But that I am bound in [[concepts/charity\|charity]] against it. |
| NORFOLK | (to Surrey)<br>Those articles, my lord, are in the [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s hand;<br>But thus much—they are foul ones. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | So much fairer<br>And spotless shall mine innocence arise<br>When the [[archetypes/king\|King]] knows my [[concepts/truth\|truth]]. |
| SURREY | This cannot save you.<br>I thank my [[motifs/memory\|memory]] I yet remember<br>Some of these articles, and out they shall.<br>Now, if you can blush and cry ‘Guilty’, Cardinal,<br>You’ll show a little honesty. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | Speak on, sir;<br>I dare your worst objections. If I blush,<br>It is to see a nobleman want manners. |
| SURREY | I had rather want those than my head. Have at you!<br>First, that without the [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s assent or knowledge<br>You wrought to be a legate, by which power<br>You maimed the jurisdiction of all bishops. |
| NORFOLK | (to Wolsey)<br>Then, that in all you writ to Rome, or else<br>To foreign princes, ‘Ego et Rex meus’<br><br>Was still inscribed—in which you brought the [[archetypes/king\|King]]<br>To be your servant. |
| SUFFOLK | (to Wolsey) Then, that without the knowledge<br>Either of [[archetypes/king\|King]] or Council, when you went<br>Ambassador to the Emperor, you made bold<br>To carry into Flanders the great seal. |
| SURREY | (to Wolsey)<br>Item, you sent a large commission<br>To Gregory de Cassado, to conclude,<br>Without the [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s will or the state’s allowance,<br>A league between his highness and Ferrara, |
| SUFFOLK | (to Wolsey)<br>That out of mere [[concepts/ambition\|ambition]] you have caused<br>Your holy hat to be stamped on the [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s coin. |
| SURREY | (to Wolsey)<br>Then, that you have sent innumerable substance—<br>By what means got, I leave to your own [[concepts/conscience\|conscience]]—<br>To furnish Rome, and to prepare the ways<br>You have for dignities to the mere undoing<br>Of all the kingdom. Many more there are,<br>Which since they are of you, and odious,<br>I will not taint my mouth with. |
| LORD CHAMBERLAIN | O, my lord,<br>Press not a falling man too far. ’Tis virtue.<br>His faults lie open to the laws. Let them,<br>Not you, correct him. My heart weeps to see him<br>So little of his great [[concepts/self\|self]]. |
| SURREY | I forgive him. |
| SUFFOLK | Lord Cardinal, the [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s further pleasure is—<br>Because all those things you have done of late,<br>By your power legantine within this kingdom,<br>Fall into th’ compass of a praemunire—<br>That therefore such a writ be sued against you,<br>To forfeit all your goods, lands, tenements,<br>Chattels, and whatsoever, and to be<br>Out of the [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s protection. This is my charge. |
| NORFOLK | (to Wolsey)<br>And so we’ll leave you to your meditations<br>How to live better. For your stubborn answer<br>About the giving back the great seal to us,<br>The [[archetypes/king\|King]] shall know it and, no [[concepts/doubt\|doubt]], shall thank you.<br>So fare you [[motifs/well\|well]], my little good lord Cardinal. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt all but Wolsey |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | So farewell—to the little good you bear me.<br>Farewell, a long farewell, to all my [[concepts/greatness\|greatness]]!<br>This is the state of man. Today he puts forth<br>The tender [[motifs/leaves\|leaves]] of hopes; tomorrow blossoms,<br>And bears his blushing honours thick upon him;<br>The third day comes a [[motifs/frost\|frost]], a killing [[motifs/frost\|frost]],<br>And when he thinks, good easy man, full surely<br>His [[concepts/greatness\|greatness]] is a-ripening, nips his root,<br>And then he falls, as I do. I have ventured,<br>Like little wanton boys that swim on bladders,<br>This many summers in a sea of glory,<br>But far beyond my depth; my high-blown [[concepts/pride\|pride]]<br>At length broke under me, and now has left me<br>Weary, and old with service, to the [[concepts/mercy\|mercy]]<br>Of a rude stream that must for ever hide me.<br>Vain pomp and glory of this world, I hate ye!<br>I feel my heart new opened. O, how wretched<br>Is that poor man that hangs on princes’ favours!<br>There is betwixt that smile we would aspire to,<br>That sweet aspect of princes, and their ruin,<br>More pangs and fears than wars or women have,<br>And when he falls, he falls like [[characters/lucifer\|Lucifer]],<br>Never to [[concepts/hope\|hope]] again. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Cromwell, who then stands amazed |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | Why, how now, Cromwell? |
| CROMWELL | I have no power to speak, sir. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | What, amazed<br>At my misfortunes? Can thy spirit [[concepts/wonder\|wonder]]<br>A great man should decline? |
| *(didascalia)* | Cromwell begins to weep |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | Nay, an you weep<br>I am fall’n indeed. |
| CROMWELL | How does your grace? |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | Why, [[motifs/well\|well]]—<br>Never so truly happy, my good Cromwell.<br>I know myself now, and I feel within me<br>A peace above all earthly dignities,<br>A still and quiet [[concepts/conscience\|conscience]]. The [[archetypes/king\|King]] has cured me.<br>I humbly thank his grace, and from these shoulders,<br>These ruined pillars, out of pity, taken<br>A load would sink a navy—too much [[concepts/honour\|honour]].<br>O, ’tis a burden, Cromwell, ’tis a burden<br>Too heavy for a man that hopes for heaven. |
| CROMWELL | I am glad your grace has made that right use of it. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | I [[concepts/hope\|hope]] I have. I am able now, methinks,<br>Out of a fortitude of [[motifs/soul\|soul]] I feel,<br>To endure more miseries and greater far<br>Than my weak-hearted enemies dare offer.<br>What news abroad? |
| CROMWELL | The heaviest and the worst<br>Is your displeasure with the [[archetypes/king\|King]]. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | God bless him. |
| CROMWELL | The next is that [[characters/sir-thomas-more\|Sir Thomas More]] is chosen<br>Lord Chancellor in your place. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | That’s somewhat sudden.<br>But he’s a learnèd man. May he continue<br>Long in his highness’ favour, and do justice<br>For [[concepts/truth\|truth]]’s sake and his [[concepts/conscience\|conscience]], that his bones,<br>When he has run his course and sleeps in blessings,<br>May have a tomb of orphans’ [[motifs/tears\|tears]] wept on him.<br>What more? |
| CROMWELL | That [[characters/cranmer\|Cranmer]] is returned with welcome,<br>Installed lord Archbishop of Canterbury. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | That’s news indeed. |
| CROMWELL | Last, that the [[characters/lady-anne\|Lady Anne]],<br>Whom the [[archetypes/king\|King]] hath in secrecy long married,<br>This day was viewed in open as his [[characters/queen\|queen]],<br>Going to chapel, and the voice is now<br>Only about her coronation. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | There was the weight that pulled me down. O,<br>Cromwell,<br>The [[archetypes/king\|King]] has gone beyond me. All my glories<br>In that one woman I have lost for ever.<br>No sun shall ever usher forth mine honours,<br>Or gild again the noble troops that waited<br>Upon my smiles. Go, get thee from me, Cromwell.<br>I am a poor fall’n man, unworthy now<br>To be thy lord and master. Seek the [[archetypes/king\|King]]—<br>That sun I pray may never set—I have told him<br>What and how true thou art. He will advance thee.<br>Some little [[motifs/memory\|memory]] of me will stir him.<br>I know his noble [[concepts/nature\|nature]] not to let<br>Thy hopeful service perish too. Good Cromwell,<br>Neglect him not. Make use now, and provide<br>For thine own future safety. |
| *(didascalia)* | CROMWELL weeping |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | O, my lord,<br>Must I then leave you? Must I needs forgo<br>So good, so noble, and so true a master?<br>Bear witness, all that have not hearts of iron,<br>With what a sorrow Cromwell [[motifs/leaves\|leaves]] his lord.<br>The [[archetypes/king\|King]] shall have my service, but my prayers<br>For ever and for ever shall be yours. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | (weeping)<br>Cromwell, I did not think to shed a tear<br>In all my miseries, but thou hast forced me,<br>Out of thy honest [[concepts/truth\|truth]], to play the woman.<br>Let’s dry our eyes, and thus far hear me, Cromwell,<br>And when I am forgotten, as I shall be,<br>And [[motifs/sleep\|sleep]] in dull cold marble, where no mention<br>Of me more must be heard of, say I taught thee—<br>Say Wolsey, that once trod the ways of glory,<br>And sounded all the depths and shoals of [[concepts/honour\|honour]],<br>Found thee a way, out of his wreck, to rise in,<br>A sure and safe one, though thy master missed it.<br>Mark but my fall, and that that ruined me.<br>Cromwell, I charge thee, fling away [[concepts/ambition\|ambition]].<br>By that sin fell the angels. How can man, then,<br>The image of his maker, [[concepts/hope\|hope]] to win by it?<br>[[motifs/love\|Love]] thyself last. Cherish those hearts that hate thee.<br>Corruption wins not more than honesty.<br>Still in thy right hand carry gentle peace<br>To [[motifs/silence\|silence]] envious tongues. Be just, and [[concepts/fear\|fear]] not.<br>Let all the ends thou aim’st at be thy country’s,<br>Thy God’s, and [[concepts/truth\|truth]]’s. Then if thou fall’st, O<br>Cromwell,<br>Thou fall‘st a blessed martyr.<br>Serve the [[archetypes/king\|King]]. And prithee, lead me in—<br>There take an inventory of all I have:<br>To the last penny ’tis the [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s. My robe,<br>And my integrity to heaven, is all<br>I dare now call mine own. O Cromwell, Cromwell,<br>Had I but served my God with half the zeal<br>I served my [[archetypes/king\|King]], He would not in mine age<br>Have left me naked to mine enemies. |
| CROMWELL | Good sir, have patience. |
| [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|CARDINAL WOLSEY]] | So I have. Farewell<br>The hopes of [[settings/court\|court]]; my hopes in heaven do dwell. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |

### Atto 4, Scena 1

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the two Gentlemen meeting one another. The first holds a paper |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | You’re [[motifs/well\|well]] met once again. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | So are you. |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | You come to take your stand here and behold<br>The [[characters/lady-anne\|Lady Anne]] pass from her coronation? |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | ’Tis all my business. At our last encounter<br>The [[characters/duke-of-buckingham\|Duke of Buckingham]] came from his trial. |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | ’Tis very true. But that [[motifs/time\|time]] offered sorrow,<br>This, general joy. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | ’Tis [[motifs/well\|well]]. The citizens,<br>I am sure, have shown at full their royal minds—<br>As, let ’em have their rights, they are ever forward—<br>In celebration of this day with shows,<br>Pageants, and sights of [[concepts/honour\|honour]]. |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | Never greater,<br>Nor, I’ll assure you, better taken, sir. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | May I be bold to ask what that contains,<br>That paper in your hand? |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | Yes, ’tis the list<br>Of those that claim their offices this day<br>By custom of the coronation.<br>The [[characters/duke-of-suffolk\|Duke of Suffolk]] is the first, and claims<br>To be High Steward; next, the Duke of Norfolk,<br>He to be Earl Marshal. You may read the rest.<br>He gives him the paper |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | I thank you, sir. Had I not known those customs,<br>I should have been beholden to your paper.<br>But I beseech you, what’s become of Katherine,<br>The Princess Dowager? How goes her business? |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | That I can tell you too. The Archbishop<br>Of Canterbury, accompanied with other<br>Learned and reverend fathers of his order,<br>Held a late [[settings/court\|court]] at Dunstable, six miles off<br>From Ampthill, where the Princess lay; to which<br>She was often cited by them, but appeared not.<br>And, to be short, for not appearance, and<br>The [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s late scruple, by the main assent<br>Of all these learned men, she was divorced,<br>And the late [[motifs/marriage\|marriage]] made of none effect,<br>Since which she was removed to Kimbolton,<br>Where she remains now sick. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | Alas, good lady! |
| *(didascalia)* | Flourish of trumpets within |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | The trumpets sound. Stand close. The [[characters/queen\|Queen]] is coming. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the coronation procession, which passes over the stage in order and state. Hautboys, within, play during the procession |
| THE ORDER OF THE | CORONATION |
| *(didascalia)* | 1. First, enter trumpeters, who play a lively flourish. |
| THE ORDER OF THE | 2. Then, enter two judges.<br>3. Then, enter the Lord Chancellor, with both the purse containing the great seal and the mace borne before him. |
| *(didascalia)* | 4. Then, enter choristers singing; with them, musicians playing. |
| THE ORDER OF THE | 5. Then, enter the [[characters/lord-mayor\|Lord Mayor]] of [[settings/london\|London]] bearing the mace, followed by Garter [[archetypes/king\|King]]-of-Arms wearing his coat of arms and a gilt copper [[motifs/crown\|crown]].<br>6. Then, enter Marquis Dorset bearing a sceptre of gold, and wearing, on his head, a demi-coronal of gold and, about his neck, a collar of esses. With him enter the [[characters/earl-of-surrey\|Earl of Surrey]] bearing the rod of silver with the dove, crowned with an earl’s coronet, and also wearing a collar of esses.<br>7. Next, enter the [[characters/duke-of-suffolk\|Duke of Suffolk]] as High Steward, in his robe of estate, with his coronet on his head, and bearing a long white wand. With him, enter the Duke of Norfolk with the rod of marshalship and a coronet on his head. Each wears a collar of esses.<br>8. Then, under a canopy borne by four barons of the Cinque Ports, enter Anne, the new [[characters/queen\|Queen]], in her robe. Her hair, which hangs loose, is richly adorned with pearl. She wears a [[motifs/crown\|crown]]. Accompanying her on either side are the Bishops of [[settings/london\|London]] and Winchester.<br>9. Next, enter the old Duchess of Norfolk, in a coronal of gold wrought with [[motifs/flowers\|flowers]], bearing the [[characters/queen\|Queen]]’s train.<br>10. Finally, enter certain ladies or countesses, with plain circlets of gold without [[motifs/flowers\|flowers]]. The two Gentlemen comment on the procession as it passes over the stage |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | A royal train, believe me. These I know.<br>Who’s that that bears the sceptre? |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | Marquis Dorset.<br>And that, the [[characters/earl-of-surrey\|Earl of Surrey]] with the rod. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | A bold brave gentleman. That should be<br>The [[characters/duke-of-suffolk\|Duke of Suffolk]]? |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | ’Tis the same: High Steward. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | And that, my lord of Norfolk? |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | Yes. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | (seeing Anne) Heaven bless thee!<br>Thou hast the sweetest face ever looked on.<br>Sir, as I have a [[motifs/soul\|soul]], she is an angel.<br>Our [[archetypes/king\|King]] has all the Indies in his arms,<br>And more, and richer, when he strains that lady.<br>I cannot blame his [[concepts/conscience\|conscience]]. |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | They that bear The cloth of [[concepts/honour\|honour]] over her are four barons<br>Of the Cinque Ports. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | Those men are happy, And so are all are near her.<br>I take it she that carries up the train<br>Is that old noble lady, Duchess of Norfolk. |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | It is. And all the rest are countesses. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | Their coronets say so. These are [[motifs/stars\|stars]] indeed— |
| *(didascalia)* | FIRST GENTLEMAN |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | And sometimes falling ones. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | No more of that. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit the last of the procession, and then |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | a great flourish of trumpets within |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter a third Gentleman in a sweat |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | God save you, sir. Where have you been broiling? |
| THIRD GENTLEMAN | Among the crowd i’th’ Abbey, where a finger<br>Could not be wedged in more. I am stifled<br>With the mere rankness of their joy. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | You saw the ceremony? |
| THIRD GENTLEMAN | That I did. |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | How Was it? |
| THIRD GENTLEMAN | [[motifs/well\|Well]] worth the seeing. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | Good sir, speak it to us. |
| THIRD GENTLEMAN | As [[motifs/well\|well]] as I am able. The rich stream<br>Of lords and ladies, having brought the [[characters/queen\|Queen]]<br>To a prepared place in the choir, fell off<br>A distance from her, while her grace sat down<br>To rest a while—some half an hour or so—<br>In a rich chair of state, opposing freely<br>The [[concepts/beauty\|beauty]] of her person to the people.<br>Believe me, sir, she is the goodliest woman<br>That ever lay by man; which when the people<br>Had the full view of, such a noise arose<br>As the shrouds make at sea in a stiff tempest,<br>As loud and to as many tunes. Hats, cloaks—<br>Doublets, I think—flew up, and had their faces<br>Been loose, this day they had been lost. Such joy<br>I never saw before. Great-bellied women,<br>That had not half a week to go, like rams<br>In the old [[motifs/time\|time]] of war, would shake the press,<br>And make ’em reel before ’em. No man living<br>Could say ‘This is my wife’ there, all were woven<br>So strangely in one piece. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | But what followed? |
| THIRD GENTLEMAN | At length her grace [[motifs/rose\|rose]], and with modest paces<br>Came to the altar, where she kneeled, and saint-like<br>Cast her fair eyes to heaven, and prayed devoutly, 86<br>Then [[motifs/rose\|rose]] again, and bowed her to the people,<br>When by the Archbishop of Canterbury<br>She had all the royal makings of a [[characters/queen\|queen]],<br>As holy oil, Edward Confessor’s [[motifs/crown\|crown]],<br>The rod and bird of peace, and all such emblems<br>Laid nobly on her. Which performed, the choir,<br>With all the choicest music of the kingdom,<br>Together sung Te Deum. So she parted,<br>And with the same full state paced back again<br>To York Place, where the feast is held. |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | Sir, You must no more call it York Place—that’s past,<br>For since the Cardinal fell, that title’s lost.<br>‘Tis now the [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s, and called Whitehall. |
| THIRD GENTLEMAN | I know it, But ’tis so lately altered that the old name<br>Is fresh about me. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | What two reverend bishops<br>Were those that went on each side of the [[characters/queen\|Queen]]? |
| THIRD GENTLEMAN | Stokesley and Gardiner, the one of Winchester—<br>Newly preferred from the [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s secretary—<br>The other [[settings/london\|London]]. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | He of Winchester<br>Is held no great good [[archetypes/lover\|lover]] of the Archbishop’s,<br>The virtuous [[characters/cranmer\|Cranmer]]. |
| THIRD GENTLEMAN | All the land knows that.<br>However, yet there is no great breach. When it<br>comes,<br>[[characters/cranmer\|Cranmer]] will find a friend will not shrink from him. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | Who may that be, I pray you? |
| THIRD GENTLEMAN | Thomas Cromwell, A man in much esteem with th’ [[archetypes/king\|King]], and truly<br>A worthy friend. The [[archetypes/king\|King]] has made him<br>Master o’th’ [[motifs/jewel\|Jewel]] House,<br>And one already of the Privy Council. |
| SECOND GENTLEMAN | He will deserve more. |
| THIRD GENTLEMAN | Yes, without all [[concepts/doubt\|doubt]].<br>Come, gentlemen, ye shall go my way,<br>Which is to th’ [[settings/court\|court]], and there ye shall be my<br>guests.<br>Something I can command. As I walk thither<br>I’ll tell ye more. |
| FIRST | and SECOND GENTLEMEN You may command us, sir. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |
| *(didascalia)* | 4.2 Three chairs. Enter Katherine Dowager, sick, led between Griffith her gentleman usher, and Patience her woman |
| GRIFFITH | How does your grace? |
| KATHERINE | O Griffith, sick to [[motifs/death\|death]].<br>My legs, like loaden branches, bow to th’ earth,<br>Willing to leave their burden. Reach a chair.<br>A chair is brought to her. She sits<br>So now, methinks, I feel a little ease.<br>Didst thou not tell me, Griffith, as thou led’st me,<br>That the great [[archetypes/child\|child]] of [[concepts/honour\|honour]], [[characters/cardinal-wolsey\|Cardinal Wolsey]],<br>Was dead? |
| GRIFFITH | Yes, madam, but I think your grace, Out of the [[motifs/pain\|pain]] you suffered, gave no ear to’t. |
| KATHERINE | Prithee, good Griffith, tell me how he died.<br>If [[motifs/well\|well]], he stepped before me happily<br>For my example. |
| GRIFFITH | [[motifs/well\|Well]], the voice goes, madam.<br>For after the stout Earl Northumberland<br>Arrested him at York, and brought him forward,<br>As a man sorely tainted, to his answer,<br>He fell sick, suddenly, and grew so ill<br>He could not sit his mule. |
| KATHERINE | Alas, poor man. |
| GRIFFITH | At last, with easy roads, he came to Leicester,<br>Lodged in the abbey, where the reverend abbot,<br>With all his convent, honourably received him,<br>To whom he gave these words: ‛O father abbot,<br>An old man broken with the storms of state<br>Is come to lay his weary bones among ye.<br>Give him a little earth, for [[concepts/charity\|charity]].’<br>So went to bed, where eagerly his sickness<br>Pursued him still, and three nights after this,<br>About the hour of eight, which he himself<br>Foretold should be his last, full of repentance,<br>Continual meditations, [[motifs/tears\|tears]], and sorrows,<br>He gave his honours to the world again,<br>His blessed part to heaven, and slept in peace. |
| KATHERINE | So may he rest, his faults lie gently on him.<br>Yet thus far, Griffith, give me leave to speak him,<br>And yet with [[concepts/charity\|charity]]. He was a man<br>Of an unbounded stomach, ever ranking<br>Himself with princes; one that by suggestion<br>Tied all the kingdom. Simony was fair play.<br>His own opinion was his law. I’th’ presence<br>He would say untruths, and be ever [[topoi/double\|double]]<br>Both in his words and meaning. He was never,<br>But where he meant to ruin, pitiful.<br>His promises were, as he then was, mighty;<br>But his performance, as he is now, nothing.<br>Of his own [[concepts/body\|body]] he was ill, and gave<br>The clergy ill example. |
| GRIFFITH | Noble madam, Men’s evil manners live in brass, their virtues<br>We write in [[motifs/water\|water]]. May it please your highness<br>To hear me speak his good now? |
| KATHERINE | Yes, good Griffith,<br>I were malicious else. |
| GRIFFITH | This cardinal, Though from an humble stock, undoubtedly<br>Was fashioned to much [[concepts/honour\|honour]]. From his cradle<br>He was a scholar, and a ripe and good one,<br>Exceeding wise, fair-spoken, and persuading;<br>Lofty and sour to them that loved him not,<br>But to those men that sought him, sweet as [[motifs/summer\|summer]].<br>And though he were unsatisfied in getting—<br>Which was a sin—yet in bestowing, madam,<br>He was most princely: ever witness for him<br>Those twins of learning that he raised in you,<br>Ipswich and [[settings/oxford\|Oxford]]—one of which fell with him,<br>Unwilling to outlive the good that did it;<br>The other, though unfinished, yet so famous,<br>So excellent in art, and still so rising,<br>That Christendom shall ever speak his virtue.<br>His overthrow heaped happiness upon him,<br>For then, and not till then, he felt himself,<br>And found the blessedness of being little.<br>And to add greater honours to his age<br>Than man could give him, he died fearing God. |
| KATHERINE | After my [[motifs/death\|death]] I wish no other herald,<br>No other speaker of my living actions<br>To keep mine [[concepts/honour\|honour]] from corruption<br>But such an honest chronicler as Griffith.<br>Whom I most hated living, thou hast made me,<br>With thy religious [[concepts/truth\|truth]] and modesty,<br>Now in his ashes [[concepts/honour\|honour]]. Peace be with him.<br>(To her woman) Patience, be near me still, and set me<br>lower.<br>I have not long to trouble thee. Good Griffith,<br>Cause the musicians play me that sad note<br>I named my knell, whilst I sit meditating<br>On that celestial harmony I go to.<br>Sad and solemn music. Katherine sleeps |
| GRIFFITH | (to the woman)<br>She is asleep. Good wench, let’s sit down quiet<br>For [[concepts/fear\|fear]] we wake her. Softly, gentle Patience.<br>They sit |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter, solemnly tripping one after another, six personages clad in white robes, wearing on their heads garlands of bays, and golden visors on their faces. They carry branches of bays or palm in their hands. They first conge unto Katherine, then [[motifs/dance\|dance]]; and, at certain changes, the first two hold a spare garland over her head at which the other four make reverent curtsies. Then the two that held the garland deliver the same to the other next two, who observe the same order in their changes and holding the garland over her head. Which done, they deliver the same garland to the last two who likewise observe the same order. At which, as it were by inspiration, she makes in her [[motifs/sleep\|sleep]] signs of rejoicing, and holdeth up her hands to heaven. And so in their dancing vanish, carrying the garland with them. The music continues |
| KATHERINE | (waking)<br>Spirits of peace, where are ye? Are ye all gone,<br>And leave me here in wretchedness behind ye?<br>Griffith and Patience rise and come forward |
| GRIFFITH | Madam, we are here. |
| KATHERINE | It is not you I call for.<br>Saw ye none enter since I slept? |
| GRIFFITH | None, madam. |
| KATHERINE | No? Saw you not even now a blessèd troop<br>Invite me to a banquet, whose bright faces<br>Cast thousand beams upon me, like the sun?<br>They promised me eternal happiness,<br>And brought me garlands, Griffith, which I feel<br>I am not worthy yet to wear. I shall,<br>Assuredly. |
| GRIFFITH | I am most joyful, madam, such good [[motifs/dreams\|dreams]]<br>Possess your fancy. |
| KATHERINE | Bid the music leave.<br>They are harsh and heavy to me. |
| *(didascalia)* | Music ceases |
| PATIENCE | (to Griffith)<br>Do you note<br>How much her grace is altered on the sudden?<br>How long her face is drawn? How pale she looks,<br>And of an earthy colour? Mark her eyes? |
| GRIFFITH | She is going, wench. Pray, pray. |
| PATIENCE | Heaven comfort her. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter a Messenger |
| MESSENGER | (to Katherine)<br>An’t like your grace— |
| KATHERINE | You are a saucy fellow—<br>Deserve we no more reverence? |
| GRIFFITH | (to the Messenger)<br>You are to blame,<br>Knowing she will not lose her wonted [[concepts/greatness\|greatness]],<br>To use so rude behaviour. Go to, kneel. |
| MESSENGER | (kneeling before Katherine)<br>I humbly do entreat your highness’ pardon.<br>My haste made me unmannerly. There is staying<br>A gentleman sent from the [[archetypes/king\|King]] to see you. |
| KATHERINE | Admit him entrance, Griffith. But this fellow<br>Let me ne’er see again. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Messenger |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Lord Caputius ushered by Griffith |
| KATHERINE | If my sight fail not,<br>You should be lord ambassador from the Emperor,<br>My royal nephew, and your name Caputius. |
| *(didascalia)* | Madam, the same, bowing your servant. |
| KATHERINE | O, my lord, The times and titles now are altered strangely<br>With me since first you knew me. But I pray you,<br>What is your pleasure with me? |
| CAPUTIUS | Noble lady, First mine own service to your grace; the next,<br>The [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s request that I would visit you,<br>Who grieves much for your weakness, and by me<br>Sends you his princely commendations,<br>And heartily entreats you take good comfort. |
| KATHERINE | O, my good lord, that comfort comes too late,<br>’Tis like a pardon after execution.<br>That gentle physic, given in [[motifs/time\|time]], had cured me;<br>But now I am past all comforts here but prayers.<br>How does his highness? |
| CAPUTIUS | Madam, in good health. |
| KATHERINE | So may he ever do, and ever flourish<br>When I shall dwell with worms, and my poor name<br>Banished the kingdom. (To her woman) Patience, is<br>that letter<br>I caused you write yet sent away? |
| PATIENCE | No, madam. |
| KATHERINE | (to Caputius)<br>Sir, I most humbly pray you to deliver<br>This to my lord the [[archetypes/king\|King]].<br>The letter is given to Caputius |
| CAPUTIUS | Most willing, madam. |
| KATHERINE | In which I have commended to his goodness<br>The model of our chaste loves, his young daughter—<br>The dews of heaven fall thick in blessings on her—<br>Beseeching him to give her virtuous breeding.<br>She is young, and of a noble modest [[concepts/nature\|nature]].<br>I [[concepts/hope\|hope]] she will deserve [[motifs/well\|well]]—and a little<br>To [[motifs/love\|love]] her for her mother’s sake, that loved him,<br>Heaven knows how dearly. My next poor petition<br>Is that his noble grace would have some pity<br>Upon my wretched women, that so long<br>Have followed both my fortunes faithfully;<br>Of which there is not one, I dare avow—<br>And now I should not lie—but will deserve,<br>For virtue and true [[concepts/beauty\|beauty]] of the [[motifs/soul\|soul]],<br>For honesty and decent carriage,<br>A right good husband. Let him be a noble,<br>And sure those men are happy that shall have ’em.<br>The last is for my men—they are the poorest,<br>But [[concepts/poverty\|poverty]] could never draw ’em from me—<br>That they may have their wages duly paid ’em,<br>And something over to remember me by.<br>If heaven had pleased to have given me longer life,<br>And able means, we had not parted thus.<br>These are the whole contents; and, good my lord,<br>By that you [[motifs/love\|love]] the dearest in this world,<br>As you wish Christian peace to souls departed,<br>Stand these poor people’s friend and urge the [[archetypes/king\|King]]<br>To do me this last rite. |
| CAPUTIUS | By heaven I will,<br>Or let me lose the fashion of a man. |
| KATHERINE | I thank you, honest lord. Remember me<br>In all [[concepts/humility\|humility]] unto his highness.<br>Say his long trouble now is passing<br>Out of this world. Tell him, in [[motifs/death\|death]] I blessed him,<br>For so I will. Mine eyes grow dim. Farewell,<br>My lord. Griffith, farewell.<br>(To her woman)<br>Nay, Patience,<br>You must not leave me yet. I must to bed.<br>Call in more women. When I am dead, good wench,<br>Let me be used with [[concepts/honour\|honour]]. Strew me over<br>With maiden [[motifs/flowers\|flowers]], that all the world may know<br>I was a chaste wife to my [[motifs/grave\|grave]]. Embalm me,<br>Then lay me forth. Although unqueened, yet like<br>A [[characters/queen\|queen]] and daughter to a [[archetypes/king\|king]] inter me.<br>I can no more. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt Caputius and Griffith at one door; |
| *(didascalia)* | Patience leading Katherine at another |

### Atto 5, Scena 1

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter at one door Gardiner, Bishop of Winchester; before him, a Page with a torch |
| GARDINER | It’s one o’[[motifs/clock\|clock]], boy, is’t not? |
| PAGE | It hath struck. |
| GARDINER | These should be hours for necessities,<br>Not for delights; times to repair our [[concepts/nature\|nature]]<br>With comforting repose, and not for us<br>To waste these times. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter at another door Sir Thomas Lovell, meeting them |
| GARDINER | Good hour of [[motifs/night\|night]], Sir Thomasl<br>Whither so late? |
| LOVELY | Came you from the [[archetypes/king\|King]], my lord? |
| GARDINER | I did, Sir Thomas, and left him at primero<br>With the [[characters/duke-of-suffolk\|Duke of Suffolk]]. |
| LOVELL | I must to him too,<br>Before he go to bed. I’ll take my leave. |
| GARDINER | Not yet, Sir Thomas Lovell—what’s the matter?<br>It seems you are in haste. An if there be<br>No great offence belongs to’t, give your friend<br>Some touch of your late business. Affairs that walk,<br>As they say spirits do, at midnight, have<br>In them a wilder [[concepts/nature\|nature]] than the business<br>That seeks dispatch by day. |
| LOVELL | My lord, I [[motifs/love\|love]] you,<br>And durst commend a secret to your ear<br>Much weightier than this work. The [[characters/queen\|Queen]]’s in labour—<br>They say in great extremity—and feared<br>She’ll with the labour end. |
| GARDINER | The fruit she goes with<br>I pray for heartily, that it may find<br>Good [[motifs/time\|time]], and live. But, for the stock, Sir Thomas,<br>I wish it grubbed up now. |
| LOVELL | Methinks I could<br>Cry the amen, and yet my [[concepts/conscience\|conscience]] says<br>She’s a good creature and, sweet lady, does<br>Deserve our better wishes. |
| GARDINER | But sir, sir,<br>Hear me, Sir Thomas. You’re a gentleman<br>Of mine own way. I know you wise, religious.<br>And let me tell you, it will ne’er be [[motifs/well\|well]]—<br>’Twill not, Sir Thomas Lovell, take’t of me—<br>Till [[characters/cranmer\|Cranmer]], Cromwell—her two hands—and she,<br>[[motifs/sleep\|Sleep]] in their graves. |
| LOVELL | Now, sir, you speak of two<br>The most remarked i‘th’ kingdom. As for Cromwell,<br>Beside that of the [[motifs/jewel\|Jewel]] House is made Master<br>O’th’ Rolls and the [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s secretary. Further, sir,<br>Stands in the gap and trade of more preferments<br>With which the [[motifs/time\|time]] will load him. Th’Archbishop<br>Is the [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s hand and tongue, and who dare speak<br>One syllable against him? |
| GARDINER | Yes, yes, Sir Thomas—<br>There are that dare, and I myself have ventured<br>To speak my mind of him, and, indeed, this day,<br>Sir—I may tell it you, I think—I have<br>Incensed the lords o’th’ Council that he is—<br>For so I know he is, they know he is—<br>A most arch heretic, a pestilence<br>That does infect the land; with which they, moved,<br>Have broken with the [[archetypes/king\|King]], who hath so far<br>Given ear to our complaint, of his great grace<br>And princely care, foreseeing those fell mischiefs<br>Our reasons laid before him, hath commanded so<br>Tomorrow morning to the Council board<br>He be convented. He’s a rank weed, Sir Thomas,<br>And we must root him out. From your affairs<br>I hinder you too long. Good [[motifs/night\|night]], Sir Thomas. |
| LOVELL | Many good nights, my lord; I rest your servant. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt Gardiner and Page at one door |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[archetypes/king\|King]] Henry and Suffolk at another door |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | (to Suffolk)<br>Charles, I will play no more tonight.<br>My mind’s not on’t. You are too hard for me. |
| SUFFOLK | Sir, I did never win of you before. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | But little, Charles,<br>Nor shall not when my fancy’s on my play.<br>Now, Lovell, from the [[characters/queen\|Queen]] what is the news? |
| LOVELL | I could not personally deliver to her<br>What you commanded me, but by her woman<br>I sent your message, who returned her thanks<br>In the great’st humbleness, and desired your highness<br>Most heartily to pray for her. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | What sayst thou? Ha?<br>To pray for her? What, is she crying out? |
| LOVELL | So said her woman, and that her suffrance made<br>Almost each pang a [[motifs/death\|death]]. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Alas, good lady. |
| SUFFOLK | God safely quit her of her burden, and<br>With gentle travail, to the gladding of<br>Your highness with an heir. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | ’Tis midnight, Charles.<br>Prithee to bed, and in thy prayers remember<br>Th’estate of my poor [[characters/queen\|queen]]. Leave me alone,<br>For I must think of that which company<br>Would not be friendly to. |
| SUFFOLK | I wish your highness<br>A quiet [[motifs/night\|night]], and my good mistress will<br>Remember in my prayers. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Charles, good [[motifs/night\|night]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Suffolk |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Sir Anthony Denny |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | [[motifs/well\|Well]], sir, what follows? |
| DENNY | Sir, I have brought my lord the Archbishop,<br>As you commanded me. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Ha, Canterbury? |
| DENNY | Ay, my good lord. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | ’Tis true—where is he, Denny? |
| DENNY | He attends your highness’ pleasure. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Bring him to us. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Denny |
| LOVELL. | (aside)<br>This is about that which the Bishop spake.<br>I am happily come hither. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter |
| LOVELL. | [[characters/cranmer\|Cranmer]]<br>the Archbishop, ushered by<br>Denny |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | (to Lovell and Denny) Avoid the gallery. |
| *(didascalia)* | Denny begins to depart. Lovell seems to stay |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Ha? I have said. Be gone.<br>What? |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt Lovell and Denny |
| [[characters/cranmer\|CRANMER]] | (aside)<br>I am fearful. Wherefore frowns he thus?<br>’Tis his aspect of terror. All’s not [[motifs/well\|well]]. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | How now, my lord? You do [[motifs/desire\|desire]] to know<br>Wherefore I sent for you. |
| [[characters/cranmer\|CRANMER]] | (kneeling) It is my duty<br>T’attend your highness’ pleasure. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Pray you, arise,<br>My good and gracious Lord of Canterbury.<br>Come, you and I must walk a turn together.<br>I have news to tell you. Come, come—give me your<br>hand. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[characters/cranmer\|Cranmer]] rises. They walk |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Ah, my good lord, I grieve at what I speak,<br>And am right sorry to repeat what follows.<br>I have, and most unwillingly, of late<br>Heard many grievous—I do say, my lord,<br>Grievous—complaints of you, which, being considered,<br>Have moved us and our Council that you shall<br>This morning come before us, where I know<br>You cannot with such [[concepts/freedom\|freedom]] purge yourself<br>But that, till further trial in those charges<br>Which will require your answer, you must take<br>Your patience to you, and be [[motifs/well\|well]] contented<br>To make your house our Tower. You a brother of us,<br>It fits we thus proceed, or else no witness<br>Would come against you. |
| [[characters/cranmer\|CRANMER]] | (kneeling)<br>I humbly thank your highness, And am right glad to catch this good occasion<br>Most throughly to be winnowed, where my chaff<br>And corn shall fly asunder. For I know<br>There’s none stands under more calumnious tongues<br>Than I myself, poor man. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] HENRY | Stand up, good Canterbury.<br>Thy [[concepts/truth\|truth]] and thy integrity is rooted<br>In us, thy friend. Give me thy hand. Stand up.<br>Prithee, let’s walk.<br>[[characters/cranmer\|Cranmer]] rises. They walk<br>Now, by my halidom,<br>What manner of man are you? My lord, I looked<br>You would have given me your petition that<br>I should have ta’en some pains to bring together<br>Yourself and your accusers, and to have heard you<br>Without indurance further. |
| [[characters/cranmer\|CRANMER]] | Most dread liege, The good I stand on is my [[concepts/truth\|truth]] and honesty.<br>If they shall fail, I with mine enemies<br>Will triumph o’er my person, which I weigh not,<br>Being of those virtues vacant. I [[concepts/fear\|fear]] nothing<br>What can be said against me. |
